TL;DR: Parsimonious Diffusion Maps (PDMs) are benchmarked against POD and convolutional autoencoder ROMs for 2D incompressible flow past a rotating cylinder, matching or exceeding AE accuracy while requiring orders of magnitude less training time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoencoders dominate nonlinear latent-space construction in data-driven reduced-order modelling, overshadowing classical manifold-learning methods grounded in numerical analysis. The authors revisit this alternative to test whether it can compete with AEs.

Method: PDMs identify a parsimonious, interpretable set of intrinsic latent coordinates and estimate their dimension directly from data. Gaussian process regression learns latent dynamics, and convex K-NN interpolation in PDM space constructs the pre-image map, for which pointwise consistency is established. Benchmarked against POD ROMs and several convolutional AE architectures on the 2D incompressible flow past a rotating cylinder (Bogdanov-Takens codimension-2 point with Hopf, saddle-node, homoclinic bifurcations).

Result: The PDM-based nonlinear ROM substantially outperforms POD-based ROMs and achieves reconstruction/prediction accuracy comparable to—and in some bifurcating regimes better than—AE-based ROMs. Latent learning with PDMs requires orders of magnitude less computational time than AE training.

Conclusion: Classical manifold-learning methods like PDMs offer a competitive, interpretable, and far more computationally efficient alternative to autoencoders for nonlinear ROM of complex bifurcating flows.

Abstract: Autoencoders (AEs) have become a dominant approach to nonlinear latent-space construction in data-driven reduced-order modelling (ROM), with their decoders lifting latent representations back to the ambient state space. Their prominence, however, has overshadowed an established alternative: manifold-learning methods grounded in classical numerical analysis. We revisit this alternative using Parsimonious Diffusion Maps (PDMs), benchmarking them against Proper Orthogonal Decomposition (POD)-based ROMs and several convolutional AE architectures for the two-dimensional incompressible flow past a rotating cylinder ---a bifurcating Navier-Stokes (NS) system organized by a codimension-2 Bogdanov-Takens point and its associated Hopf, saddle-node, and homoclinic bifurcations. Our approach uses PDMs to identify a parsimonious and interpretable set of intrinsic latent coordinates and to estimate their dimension directly from data. Gaussian process regression then learns the latent dynamics, while convex K-nearest-neighbor (K-NN) interpolation in PDMs space constructs the pre-image map, for which we establish pointwise consistency. The resulting nonlinear ROM substantially outperforms POD-based ROMs and achieves reconstruction and prediction accuracy comparable to ---and, in some bifurcating regimes, better than--- that of AE-based ROMs. At the same time, latent-variable learning with PDMs requires orders of magnitude less computational time than AE training.

</details>


### [29] [Morley element for the linear strain gradient elasticity problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24340)
*Xiao Li,Shudan Tian,Hua Wang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A discrete H^1-Korn inequality is established for FE spaces with second-order weak continuity, yielding a parameter-free stabilized Morley method for strain gradient elasticity that is robust in λ and ι.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strain gradient elasticity typically necessitates stabilization or penalty parameters, and classical H^2-nonconforming elements lack a guaranteed discrete Korn inequality, complicating robustness with respect to material parameters.

Method: Prove a discrete H^1-Korn inequality for finite element spaces with a second-order weak continuity condition, showing classical H^2-nonconforming elements satisfy it. Use this to build a stabilized Morley method for linear strain gradient elasticity with piecewise quadratics, avoiding penalty parameters.

Result: The method requires no penalty parameter and is proven robust with respect to both λ and ι; numerical experiments confirm the theory.

Conclusion: The discrete H^1-Korn inequality enables a clean, parameter-free stabilized Morley scheme for strain gradient elasticity that is uniformly robust.

Abstract: We establish a discrete $H^1$-Korn inequality for finite element spaces satisfying a second order weak continuity condition. This implies that classical $H^2$-nonconforming elements satisfy the discrete Korn inequality. Based on this result, we develop a stabilized Morley finite element method for linear strain gradient elasticity using only piecewise quadratic polynomials. Thanks to the discrete $H^1$-Korn inequality, this method does not require any penalty parameter. We prove that the method is robust with respect to both $λ$ and $ι$. Numerical experiments confirm the theoretical results.

</details>


### [30] [The transposition method in saddle-point problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24478)
*Dietmar Gallistl,Zhen Liu*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper extends the transposition method from second-order elliptic PDEs to saddle-point problems under Brezzi splitting and elliptic regularity mapping assumptions, providing a framework for approximation by standard Galerkin-type methods with possibly nonconforming data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The transposition method is well established for second-order elliptic PDEs but not for saddle-point problems. The authors aim to generalize it to saddle-point settings, enabling approximation via standard Galerkin methods even for irregular data, without requiring data regularization.

Method: They formulate the transposition method for saddle-point problems using structural assumptions from Brezzi splitting combined with mapping properties reflecting elliptic regularity for concrete differential operators with boundary conditions. A framework is devised granting approximation by standard, unmodified Galerkin-type methods, which may be nonconforming with respect to the more general data allowed by the transposed operator.

Result: The framework covers several examples, including the Laplacian, Stokes operator, linear elasticity, the bi-Laplacian, and the stationary Maxwell problem with L^2 boundary data.

Conclusion: The saddle-point approach offers the advantage that data need not be regularized. The proposed framework successfully generalizes transposition to saddle-point problems and applies to a wide range of differential operators.

Abstract: The transposition method known from the theory of second-order elliptic partial differential equations is formulated for saddle-point problems under certain structural assumptions known from the Brezzi splitting plus mapping properties that reflect elliptic regularity in concrete instances of differential operators with boundary conditions. A framework is devised that grants approximation by standard, unmodified Galerkin-type methods, which may be nonconforming with respect to the more general data allowed by the transposed operator. That the data need not be regularized is an advantage particular to the saddle-point approach. Examples covered by the framework include the Laplacian, the Stokes operator, linear elasticity, the bi-Laplacian, and the stationary Maxwell problem with $L^2$ boundary data.

</details>


### [31] [Numerical Realization of an Entropy-Based Selection Principle in the Example of Kelvin-Helmholtz Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24479)
*Shaoshuai Chu,Michael Herty*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper extends parameterized Young-measure/linear-programming schemes to 2D and applies them to the Kelvin-Helmholtz instability of the 2D Euler equations, showing these schemes consistently yield the largest physical entropy among the compared methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend numerical schemes based on parameterized Young measures and linear programming to 2D and test their feasibility and entropy-selection behavior on a challenging flow problem, the Kelvin-Helmholtz instability.

Method: Construct first- to ninth-order Young-measure schemes in two space dimensions, comparing them with standard LLF flux-splitting schemes and LLF with entropy-based local characteristic decomposition (LLF-ELCD). Analyze density profiles, time/space averages, regional empirical distributions, averaged density marginals, and dissipative weak-solution selection criteria.

Result: Results show scheme-dependent flow patterns, especially in small-scale roll-up structures. Young-measure schemes yield the largest time-averaged physical entropy at every order, while LLF-ELCD does not systematically exceed standard LLF. Averaged density marginals concentrate on neighboring density states, and later cumulative averages converge across Young-measure schemes.

Conclusion: The 2D Young-measure formulation is feasible, and the linear-programming objective function acts as an effective selection mechanism. The entropy preference aligns with local optimization of expected physical entropy and cannot be reproduced by simply adding entropy to a conventional numerical construction.

Abstract: In this paper, we extend numerical schemes based on parameterized Young measures and linear programming to two space dimensions and apply them to the Kelvin-Helmholtz instability governed by the two-dimensional Euler equations of gas dynamics. We construct first-, second-, third-, fifth-, seventh-, and ninth-order Young-measure schemes and compare them with corresponding standard local Lax-Friedrichs (LLF) flux-splitting schemes and LLF schemes equipped with an entropy-based local characteristic decomposition (LLF-ELCD). We examine instantaneous and time-averaged density profiles, cumulative averages across spatial reconstruction orders, regional empirical density distributions, averaged density marginals, and several selection criteria for dissipative weak solutions. The numerical results reveal scheme-dependent flow patterns, particularly in the small-scale structures generated during the roll-up of the shear layers. At every reconstruction order considered, the Young-measure schemes yield the largest time-averaged physical entropy. By contrast, the LLF-ELCD schemes do not systematically yield larger entropy than the standard LLF schemes and therefore do not constitute a consistent maximum-entropy selection mechanism. The averaged density marginals of the Young measures are concentrated on several neighboring density states, and the later cumulative averages are closer for all Young-measure schemes. These results demonstrate the feasibility of the two-dimensional Young-measure formulation and show that the objective function in the linear-programming problem can act as an effective selection mechanism. The observed entropy preference is consistent with the local optimization of the expected physical entropy. It cannot be reproduced by merely incorporating entropy into a conventional numerical construction.

</details>


### [32] [Error Analysis and Precision Selection for Mixed-Precision DEIM-CUR Decompositions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24509)
*Ioannis Thanasis,Erin Carson*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how an inexact SVD and finite precision arithmetic affect the quality of CUR matrix approximations, particularly the DEIM-CUR method, and provides precision-selection guidelines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing bounds on CUR approximation quality typically assume all or part of the SVD is computed exactly, which is unrealistic in practice. The authors want to relax this assumption and understand how inexact computations and finite precision affect accuracy.

Method: The authors theoretically analyze the impact of an inexact SVD on CUR approximation quality and study the effect of finite precision arithmetic on the DEIM-CUR method. They derive guidelines for selecting working precisions for both the SVD and the DEIM index-selection algorithm.

Result: The analysis shows that in many cases one can use very low precisions for the SVD and DEIM computations without significantly impacting approximation quality. Numerical experiments support the theoretical findings.

Conclusion: Relaxing the exact-SVD assumption yields practical precision guidelines, suggesting low-precision computation is often sufficient for CUR/DEIM-CUR approximations.

Abstract: CUR approximation is a popular approach for constructing an interpretable low-rank approximation of a matrix. There are many approaches for constructing the CUR approximation, many of which are based on the SVD. Results in the literature bounding the quality of the approximation usually rely on the assumption that all or part of the SVD is computed exactly. In this work, we relax this assumption and show how the quality of the approximation is affected by an inexact SVD. Moreover, we analyze the effect of finite precision arithmetic on the DEIM-CUR method. Our analysis suggests guidelines for selecting the working precisions for the SVD and the DEIM index selection algorithm. In particular, it suggests that in many cases one can use very low precisions for these computations without significantly impacting the approximation quality. A set of numerical experiments is presented to demonstrate the theoretical results.

</details>


### [33] [A priori regularity of the reverse heat flow and dimension-dependent complexity of higher-order diffusion samplers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24622)
*Xixian Wang,Zhongjian Wang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper proves arbitrary-order, dimension-free a priori regularity estimates for the reverse heat flow (probability-flow ODE) in score-based diffusion models with possibly singular, compactly supported targets, and applies them to bound the convergence of Taylor and Runge-Kutta samplers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reverse heat flow / probability-flow ODEs underpin score-based diffusion sampling, but existing regularity guarantees are limited in order and depend poorly on dimension, especially for singular targets. The authors aim to establish high-order, dimension-uniform regularity so that high-order numerical samplers can be rigorously analyzed.

Method: Under a dimension-uniform doubling condition on supporting-cap masses for compactly supported, possibly singular targets, the authors bound iterated material derivatives of the flow and their first spatial derivatives pointwise, with constants independent of dimension d and explicit in time and support radius. Key tools: a spatially uniform bound on normal-direction posterior fluctuations (the only place target geometry enters) and a centered posterior-moment normal form closed under material differentiation, combined with a weighted regularity calculus, yielding bounds at every order including mixed space-time directional derivatives needed for Runge-Kutta stages.

Result: For exact-score sampling stopped at forward time δ>0, order-p Taylor and explicit Runge-Kutta schemes reach accuracy ε in Õ(d^{1/p} ε^{-1/p}) steps in total variation, and the Taylor scheme in Õ(d^{1/(2p)} ε^{-1/p}) steps in W_2.

Conclusion: The authors deliver dimension-free, arbitrary-order regularity estimates for the reverse heat flow and translate them into explicit step-complexity guarantees for high-order samplers in total variation and Wasserstein distance.

Abstract: We establish arbitrary-order a priori regularity estimates for the reverse heat flow in the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck setting, that is, for the probability-flow ODE of score-based diffusion models. For compactly supported, possibly singular targets satisfying a dimension-uniform doubling condition on supporting-cap masses, we bound the iterated material derivatives of the flow and their first spatial derivatives pointwise, with constants independent of the dimension $d$ and explicit in time and support radius. The proof rests on two ingredients: a spatially uniform bound on normal-direction posterior fluctuations, which is the only point where the geometry of the target enters, and a centered posterior-moment normal form closed under material differentiation. This algebraic structure, together with a weighted regularity calculus, yields quantitative bounds at every order, including the mixed space--time directional derivatives required by Runge--Kutta stages. As an application, for exact-score sampling stopped at forward time $δ>0$, order-$p$ Taylor and explicit Runge--Kutta schemes reach accuracy $\varepsilon$ in $\widetilde O(d^{1/p}\varepsilon^{-1/p})$ steps in total variation, and the Taylor scheme in $\widetilde O(d^{1/(2p)}\varepsilon^{-1/p})$ steps in $W_2$.

</details>


### [34] [Edge-based Katz centralities for spatio-temporal multiplex networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24638)
*Kai Bergermann,Francesco Gravili,Valeria Simoncini,Martin Stoll*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces a failure-aware temporal multiplex network model for computing edge-based Katz centralities in spatial networks, using a block-triangular/banded supra-adjacency matrix, with truncation approaches yielding up to 679x runtime gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Katz centrality is well-established for node ranking via linear system solve, but existing temporal Katz centrality notions suffer from instability (small structural changes drastically alter centralities) and edge-based measures remain underexplored. This work targets edge-based Katz centrality for spatial networks.

Method: Uses the line graph as network representation for spatial networks. Employs a block-triangular and -banded supra-adjacency matrix with inter-layer connections modeled as low-rank matrices, applying spatially constrained emphasis on up-/downdated edges (e.g., pipe breakages, street obstructions). Analyzes block matrix inverse entry decay above the block-diagonal, yielding two truncation approaches with controlled truncation error.

Result: Numerical experiments on real-world spatio-temporal networks up to 2·10^8 size demonstrate accuracy and efficiency, with runtime gains up to a factor of 679.

Conclusion: The failure-aware temporal multiplex model and truncation approaches enable efficient, accurate edge-based Katz centrality computation for large-scale spatial networks, supporting real-time infrastructure monitoring applications.

Abstract: Katz centrality is a well-established measure to identify and rank the most important nodes in complex networks by means of a linear system solve. Recent works have developed notions of Katz centrality for temporal, i.e., time-evolving networks. Their drawback is that small changes in the network structure may drastically change centralities across large parts of the network. Moreover, an unproportional effort in the network science community has been devoted to the study of node-based quantities while edge-based measures are far less explored. In this manuscript, we introduce a novel failure-aware temporal multiplex network model for computing edge-based Katz centralities for spatial networks. This class of networks admits the use of the line graph as network representation. We use a block-triangular and -banded supra-adjacency matrix representation, modeling inter-layer connections as low-rank matrices, which assigns a spatially constrained emphasis on up- or downdated edges. These could represent, e.g., breakages of water/gas pipes or obstructions in street networks in real-time infrastructure monitoring applications. We analyze the structure of the of the block matrix inverse with respect to entry decay in the blocks above the block-diagonal. This gives rise to two truncation approaches that allow drastic computational runtime reductions at the cost of introducing a controlled truncation error. Numerical experiments on a range of real-world spatio-temporal networks of size up to $2\cdot 10^8$ illustrate accuracy and efficiency with runtime gains of up to a factor of $679$.

</details>


### [35] [Fourth-Order Energy-Consistent Average Vector Field Discrete Gradient Methods for Dissipative Hamiltonian Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24692)
*Lucas Lautwein,Nicole Marheineke,Håkon Noren Myhr,Kevin Schäfers*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper extends the perturbation principle to dissipative Hamiltonian systems, deriving fourth-order average vector field discrete gradient schemes that preserve energy dissipativity via congruence-based and exponential completions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional discrete gradient methods for dissipative Hamiltonian systems are structure-preserving and energy-consistent but limited to at most second-order convergence, restricting their accuracy.

Method: The authors extend the perturbation principle (previously for Poisson systems) to the dissipative setting. They use a congruence-based completion that preserves positive semi-definiteness of the dissipation matrix, guaranteeing unconditional energy dissipativity. An alternative exponential completion with structure-preserving rational approximations is derived for purely dissipative systems, characterizing Padé approximants that preserve positive semi-definiteness for use in energy-associated splitting schemes.

Result: Numerical experiments on a damped physical pendulum and a dissipative Fermi--Pasta--Ulam system show the positive semi-definite completion avoids nonphysical instabilities and captures correct qualitative dynamics. The methods achieve desired accuracy and energy consistency at competitive cost, with fourth-order schemes outperforming the conventional second-order discrete gradient method in efficiency.

Conclusion: The completion concept offers a systematic route to higher-order energy-consistent schemes for dissipative Hamiltonian systems, with fourth-order schemes proving computationally more efficient than conventional second-order methods.

Abstract: Discrete gradient methods provide a structure-preserving approach to energy-consistent time integration of dissipative Hamiltonian systems. In their conventional formulation, however, they generally attain at most second-order convergence. Extending the perturbation principle previously developed for Poisson systems to the dissipative setting, we derive fourth-order average vector field discrete gradient schemes. A congruence-based completion preserves the positive semi-definiteness of the dissipation matrix, thereby guaranteeing unconditional energy dissipativity for all positive step sizes. This completion concept provides a systematic route to higher-order energy-consistent schemes. For purely dissipative systems, an alternative exponential completion is derived, together with structure-preserving rational approximations. In particular, we characterize Padé approximants that preserve positive semi-definiteness and can be incorporated into energy-associated splitting schemes. Numerical experiments on a damped physical pendulum and a dissipative Fermi--Pasta--Ulam system demonstrate the importance of the positive semi-definite completion for avoiding nonphysical numerical instabilities and capturing the correct qualitative dynamics. They further show that the proposed methods achieve the desired accuracy and energy consistency at a computational cost competitive with existing Galerkin-type approaches. Moreover, the fourth-order schemes outperform the conventional second-order discrete gradient method in computational efficiency over the considered accuracy range, despite their higher per-step cost.

</details>


### [36] [Constraints on admissible behavior of GMRES applied to tridiagonal Toeplitz systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24751)
*Fei Chen,Kirk M. Soodhalter*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper studies admissible and attainable GMRES behavior for non-Hermitian tridiagonal Toeplitz matrices, showing that Toeplitz structure constrains—but does not eliminate—the continuum of possible convergence curves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Greenbaum and Strakoš showed any convergence curve is possible for a given eigenvalue set, and Arioli, Pták, and Strakoš parameterized matrix–right-hand-side pairs, proving GMRES behavior cannot be characterized by eigenvalues alone. This raises the question of how such theory manifests under constrained matrix structure, which the paper addresses for tridiagonal Toeplitz matrices.

Method: The authors apply the Greenbaum–Strakoš and Arioli–Pták–Strakoš theory to matrices with constrained structure, focusing on non-Hermitian (non-symmetric) tridiagonal Toeplitz matrices, analyzing how Toeplitz structure restricts the achievable matrix–right-hand-side pairs and resulting GMRES convergence.

Result: Toeplitz structure necessarily constrains how the prior theory can manifest, but a continuum of admissible GMRES behaviors is still attainable for such matrices.

Conclusion: Matrix structure imposes real restrictions on GMRES behavior beyond eigenvalue information, yet tridiagonal Toeplitz matrices still admit a continuum of admissible convergence behaviors.

Abstract: The result of Greenbaum and Strakoš that for a given set of eigenvalues, any convergence curve is possible [SIMAX 1996] and the subsequent parameterization of such matrix-right-hand side pairs $(A,\mathbf{b})$ of Arioli, Pták, and Strakoš [BIT 1998] demonstrated that the behavior of the \gmres could not be completely characterized by the eigenvalues of $A$ alone. In this paper, we consider how to use this theory to understand the admissible and attainable \gmres behavior for matrices with constrained structure, focussing on non-Hermitian (non-symmetric) tridiagonal Toeplitz matrices. We show that Toeptliz structure necessarily constrains the how the theory from these papers can manifest but that a continuum of admissible behaviors is still attainable.

</details>


### [37] [An adaptive localized orthogonal decomposition method for Helmholtz problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24776)
*Yuwen Li,Chuxin Que*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces an Adaptive Localized Orthogonal Decomposition (ALOD) method for Helmholtz problems with localized oscillations/singularities, delivering the first wavenumber-robust residual a posteriori error estimate for Helmholtz LOD within the standard resolution/oversampling/stability regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Helmholtz problems with localized oscillations and singularities demand adaptive discretizations. Existing LOD-based error estimators are not wavenumber-robust, and adaptation of coarse space, fine corrector mesh, and oversampling is entangled. A robust, two-sided estimator enabling separate adaptation is needed.

Method: Residual-based a posteriori estimator (two-sided, wavenumber-robust, with computable corrector-localization error bound). Local residual Riesz representatives reused as regional additive Schwarz corrections to form a shared enrichment, compressed by energy-weighted POD for online use. Kernel-lifted tests ensure stable quasi-optimal coupling under a unified oversampling condition without extra global fine-grid solves; memberwise checks control compression perturbation.

Result: First wavenumber-robust residual a posteriori estimate for Helmholtz LOD in the standard regime; two-sided relative to the fine-grid solution; enables separate adaptation of online coarse space, fine corrector mesh, and oversampling. Two-dimensional experiments show online compression and low-rank reuse for prescribed load families.

Conclusion: The adaptive error-control mechanism directly generates the reduced space, and the estimate remains valid for enriched solutions satisfying Galerkin orthogonality on the original LOD test space, yielding an efficient online compression scheme with low-rank reuse.

Abstract: We develop an adaptive localized orthogonal decomposition (ALOD) method for Helmholtz problems with localized oscillations and singularities. The main result is the first wavenumber-robust residual a posteriori error estimate for a Helmholtz LOD discretization within the standard resolution, oversampling, and discrete-stability regime. The estimate is two-sided relative to the fine-grid solution and includes a computable bound for the corrector-localization error, enabling separate adaptation of the online coarse space, the fine corrector mesh, and the oversampling level. For a prescribed load family, we reuse the local residual Riesz representatives already computed by the indicator as regional additive Schwarz corrections. Their span forms a shared enrichment, which is then compressed by energy-weighted proper orthogonal decomposition (POD) for online use. Thus, the reduced space is generated directly by the adaptive error-control mechanism. The error estimate remains valid for enriched solutions satisfying Galerkin orthogonality on the original LOD test space. Kernel-lifted tests provide stable, quasi-optimal coupling under a unified oversampling condition without additional global fine-grid solves, and memberwise checks control the perturbation caused by compression. Two-dimensional experiments demonstrate online compression and low-rank reuse for the prescribed load families.

</details>


### [38] [A Domain Derivative for Electromagnetic Scattering by Perfect Conductors in the Time Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24822)
*Marvin Knöller*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper establishes a domain derivative for time-dependent electromagnetic scattering from perfect conductors and uses it in an iterative shape reconstruction algorithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Shape reconstruction in time-dependent electromagnetic scattering requires a domain derivative (shape sensitivity), but deriving and numerically approximating it rigorously is challenging due to the time-dependent Maxwell system and its regularity/convergence properties.

Method: The authors proceed through the Laplace domain to derive frequency-dependent bounds on Maxwell solutions, use them to establish time regularity of the domain derivative, prove convergence of a Runge-Kutta convolution quadrature time semi-discretization, and combine this with a Galerkin space discretization with full convergence analysis for pointwise evaluations.

Result: Convergence of the Runge-Kutta convolution quadrature semi-discretization is proven, and full convergence analysis for pointwise domain derivative evaluations is carried out. The derivative drives an iterative shape reconstruction algorithm using electric near-field measurements at receiver positions; numerical examples show feasibility and robustness to added noise.

Conclusion: A rigorous, computable domain derivative for time-dependent electromagnetic scattering is developed and successfully applied to robust iterative shape reconstruction from near-field data.

Abstract: A domain derivative for time-dependent electromagnetic scattering from perfect conductors is established. By proceeding through the Laplace domain, frequency-dependent bounds on solutions to Maxwell's equations are derived. These bounds are used both for establishing time regularity properties of the domain derivative and for proving convergence of the proposed Runge--Kutta convolution quadrature semi-discretization in time. A full convergence analysis is also carried out for pointwise evaluations of the domain derivative, when this time discretization is combined with a Galerkin method in space. Eventually, the domain derivative is applied in an iterative shape reconstruction algorithm, in which measurements of the electric near field at some receiver positions, away from the perfect conductor are measured. Numerical examples show the feasibility of this algorithm and in particular highlight its robustness, when additional noise is applied to the data.

</details>


### [39] [GradAgent: A Knowledge-Guided Multi-Agent System for Structure-Preserving Gradient-Flow Computation with an Application to Multicomponent Vesicle Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24871)
*Zhenlin Guo,Jiale Meng,Shuqi Tang,Haiyan Su,Maosheng Jiang,Kaiwen Shi,Meng Zhao*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: GradAgent is a knowledge-guided multi-agent system that coordinates model analysis, algorithm design/proofs, and numerical validation to construct conservative, energy-stable schemes for coupled gradient-flow systems, demonstrated on a multicomponent vesicle phase-field-fluid model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-order differential operators and nonlinear coupling make it hard to build conservative and energy-stable schemes for coupled gradient-flow systems. Existing approaches lack a way to reuse structured knowledge and independently verify proofs across stages.

Method: A multi-agent system with three coordinated agents (model analysis; algorithm design and proofs; numerical implementation and validation) plus independent audits to catch mathematical errors and proof gaps. Two modes: Reconstruction Mode rebuilds 20 published studies into an extensible knowledge graph (GradAgent-KG) linking model structures, discretizations, proofs, implementations, and numerical evidence; Design Mode assesses retrieved knowledge applicability and creates new schemes.

Result: Applied to a fully coupled multicomponent vesicle phase-field-fluid model, GradAgent yields three first-order and three second-order schemes across three families (four linear, decoupled). All six conserve membrane mass and vesicle volume and unconditionally dissipate discrete energy under stated assumptions. KG use increases diversity in structure-preserving schemes. Numerical tests confirm second-order spatial accuracy, expected temporal orders, conservation, energy dissipation; 3D shear-flow simulations agree with experiments.

Conclusion: GradAgent shows that combining reusable knowledge, coordinated reasoning, and independent auditing can develop and validate structure-preserving algorithms for complex coupled systems.

Abstract: High-order differential operators and nonlinear coupling make it challenging to construct conservative and energy-stable schemes for coupled gradient-flow systems. We present GradAgent, a knowledge-guided multi-agent system that coordinates three agents across model analysis, algorithm design and proofs, and numerical implementation and validation. Independent audits strengthen reliability by uncovering mathematical errors and proof gaps, guiding revisions, and maintaining consistency across stages. In Reconstruction Mode, GradAgent reconstructs 20 published studies and organizes audited knowledge in an extensible knowledge graph (KG), GradAgent-KG, linking model structures, discretization strategies and proofs, implementations, and numerical evidence. In Design Mode, the agents assess the applicability of retrieved knowledge and develop new schemes informed by relevant discretization strategies. Applied to the fully coupled multicomponent vesicle phase-field-fluid model, GradAgent yields three first-order and three second-order schemes across three algorithmic families, including four linear, decoupled schemes. Under stated assumptions, all six schemes conserve membrane component mass and vesicle volume and dissipate their respective temporally discrete energies unconditionally. Comparisons with and without GradAgent-KG show that it promotes diversity in structure-preserving scheme design for this target model. Numerical tests confirm second-order spatial accuracy, the expected temporal orders, conservation, and temporally discrete energy dissipation, while three-dimensional shear-flow simulations agree qualitatively with experiments. These results demonstrate GradAgent's ability to combine reusable knowledge, coordinated reasoning, and independent auditing to develop and validate structure-preserving algorithms for complex coupled systems.

</details>


### [40] [A Contour Method for Multiparameter Eigenvalue Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24943)
*Emil Graf,Alex Townsend*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper introduces the first contour method for analytic multiparameter eigenvalue problems, based on a new residue formula for multivariate matrix-valued analytic functions generalizing Beyn's residue theorem, enabling computation of all eigenvalues in a prescribed region of C^d.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multiparameter eigenvalue problems are important in boundary value problems, stability analysis, and delay-differential equations, but existing methods either require solving extremely large global problems or rely on local iterative techniques that recover only a few eigenvalues at a time.

Method: The authors develop a new residue formula for multivariate matrix-valued analytic functions, extending Beyn's Keldysh-based residue theorem for meromorphic operator functions to several complex variables. Using this, they derive a multidimensional analogue of Beyn's contour method that computes all eigenvalues of a multiparameter eigenvalue problem within a prescribed region of C^d, without constructing enormous matrices and being embarrassingly parallelizable.

Result: Numerical experiments show that large multiparameter problems arising in applications can now be successfully solved, where existing approaches struggle.

Conclusion: The proposed contour method provides an efficient, parallelizable approach for solving analytic multiparameter eigenvalue problems, overcoming the limitations of prior global and local methods.

Abstract: Multiparameter eigenvalue problems arise in boundary value problems, stability analysis, and delay-differential equations. Despite their importance, existing methods either require solving extremely large global problems or rely on local iterative techniques that only recover a few eigenvalues at a time. In this work we develop the first contour method for analytic multiparameter eigenvalue problems. The key theoretical ingredient is a new residue formula for multivariate matrix-valued analytic functions, extending Beyn's Keldysh-based residue theorem for meromorphic operator functions to several complex variables. Using this, we derive a multidimensional analogue of Beyn's contour method that computes all the eigenvalues of a multiparameter eigenvalue problem contained in a prescribed region of $\mathbb{C}^d$. The resulting algorithm targets eigenvalues in a region without constructing preposterously large matrices and is embarrassingly parallelizable. Numerical experiments demonstrate that we can now successfully solve large multiparameter problems arising in applications where existing approaches struggle.

</details>

### [41] [Absence of gelation and regularity for coagulation-fragmentation-diffusion equations with critical coagulation rate in low dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22399)
*Hector Bouton*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proves mass conservation (no gelation) for a discrete coagulation-fragmentation-diffusion system: in dimension d≤2 under the critical additive coagulation bound, and in dimension d≤4 under higher decay rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To close the remaining gap between the spatially homogeneous setting and the spatially inhomogeneous diffusive setting regarding mass conservation and gelation for coagulation-fragmentation systems.

Method: Analyzes a discrete coagulation-fragmentation-diffusion system on a bounded domain of R^d. Uses the critical additive bound a_{i,j} ≤ C(i+j) for d≤2, and imposes a higher decay rate on coagulation and fragmentation coefficients (with mild diffusion-rate assumptions) for d≤4.

Result: In d≤2, mass conservation (absence of gelation) is proven under the critical additive bound. In d≤4, regularity and absence of gelation are proven under higher decay rate and mild diffusion assumptions.

Conclusion: The critical additive bound result closes the gap between the homogeneous and inhomogeneous diffusive settings, extending mass conservation and regularity results to higher dimensions.

Abstract: We consider a discrete coagulation-fragmentation-diffusion system on a bounded domain of $\mathbb{R}^d$. In dimension $d\leq 2$, we prove mass conservation (that is absence of gelation) when the coagulation coefficient satisfies the critical additive bound $a_{i, j} \leq C(i + j)$ for some constant $C > 0$. We thereby close the remaining gap between the spatially homogeneous setting and the spatially inhomogeneous diffusive setting. In dimension $d\leq 4$, we impose a higher decay rate on the coagulation and fragmentation coefficients, but we nonetheless prove regularity and absence of gelation under mild assumptions on the diffusion rates.

</details>


### [42] [A Supercritical Problem in Dimension Two](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22413)
*Manuel del Pino,Ignacio Guerra,Monica Musso*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For a symmetric bounded domain and subcritical exponents, the paper constructs positive solutions to $-Δu=λu e^{u^{2+ε}}$ that blow up at the origin as a tower of k separated Liouville bubbles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The equation $-Δu=λu e^{u^{2+ε}}$ is a subcritical approximation of the critical exponential (Trudinger-Moser type) problem. Understanding blow-up behavior with multiple bubbles at a single point is of interest, and prior work typically handles single bubbles or less separated scales.

Method: Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction adapted to handle bubbles whose concentration scales are strongly separated.

Result: For every fixed integer k≥1 and all sufficiently small ε>0, there exists a positive solution blowing up at the origin as a tower of k Liouville bubbles with strongly separated concentration scales.

Conclusion: Multi-bubble tower solutions exist for the subcritical exponential problem in symmetric domains, with a reduction scheme accommodating distinct scales.

Abstract: Let $Ω\subset\mathbb R^2$ be a smooth bounded domain containing the origin and invariant under reflection across the coordinate axes, and let $0<λ<λ_1(Ω)$, where $Λ_1 (Ω)$ is the first eigenvalue for $-Δ$ on $Ω$ under Dirichlet boundary conditions. For every fixed integer $k\ge1$ and all sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, we construct a positive solution of \[ -Δu=λu e^{u^{2+\varepsilon}} \quad\hbox{in }Ω, \qquad u=0\quad\hbox{on }\partialΩ, \] which blows up at the origin as a tower of $k$ Liouville bubbles. The concentration scales are strongly separated. The proof is based on a Lyapunov--Schmidt reduction adapted to these different scales.

</details>


### [43] [On the Zero-Mass Problem of Bandle, Levine, and Zhang](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22458)
*Mohamed Jleli,Bessem Samet*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Resolves a 2000 open problem on the inhomogeneous semilinear heat equation with zero-mass perturbation, proving nonexistence of global weak solutions for 1<p≤N/(N-2).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bandle, Levine, and Zhang (2000) posed the zero-mass case as an open problem. Previously only the positive-mass case was understood; the zero-mass case had remained unresolved.

Method: Analyzes the critical behavior of u_t − Δu = |u|^p + w(x) in (0,∞)×R^N, with N≥3, p>1, and w∈L^1(R^N) nontrivial with zero total mass (∫w=0). Proves nonexistence of global weak solutions via a suitable argument for 1<p≤N/(N-2).

Result: Nonexistence of global weak solutions for 1<p≤N/(N-2). Combined with known supercritical small-data existence, the critical exponent remains p_c=N/(N-2), the same as in the positive-mass case.

Conclusion: The critical exponent separating nonexistence and existence regimes is p_c=N/(N-2) even in the zero-mass case, fully answering the open problem.

Abstract: We study the critical behavior of the inhomogeneous semilinear heat equation \[ u_t-Δu=|u|^p+w(x) \qquad\text{in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^N, \] where \(N\geq3\), \(p>1\), and \(w\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^N)\) is nontrivial and has zero total mass: \[ \int_{\mathbb{R}^N}w(x)\,dx=0. \] This zero-mass case was posed as an open problem by Bandle, Levine, and Zhang in 2000 and has remained unresolved since then. We provide a complete answer to this problem by proving nonexistence of global weak solutions for \[ 1<p\leq\frac{N}{N-2}. \] Combined with the known supercritical existence result for sufficiently small data, this shows that the critical exponent separating the nonexistence and existence regimes remains \[ p_c=\frac{N}{N-2}, \] the same as in the positive-mass case.

</details>


### [44] [Smooth and swirling steady vortex rings near the Hill--Norbury family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22513)
*Răzvan-Octavian Radu,Noah Stevenson*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The small-flux Hill–Norbury family of nonsmooth axisymmetric traveling-wave vortex rings is shown to be nowhere isolated from smooth vortex rings: each member is a limit of infinitely smooth traveling-wave Euler solutions with compact toroidal vorticity support, both without and with swirl.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hill's zero-flux spherical vortex and Norbury's small-flux vortex rings form an idealized family of swirl-free, nonsmooth axisymmetric traveling-wave Euler solutions whose vorticity is a scaled characteristic function of the compact core. The paper confronts the desingularization question: whether these singular objects can be approximated by smooth traveling-wave solutions, within the swirl-free subspace and upon admitting swirl.

Method: A five-dimensional reformulation of the Stokes stream function, combined with delicate thin-domain analysis, singular-parameter Newton-type nonlinear inversion, and topological fixed-point arguments.

Result: Each sufficiently small-flux member of the Hill–Norbury family is a limit of traveling-wave Euler solutions that are smooth to infinite order with compact toroidal vorticity support. In the swirling regime, a curious flexibility appears: total vorticity may diverge along sequences converging in a natural desingularization topology.

Conclusion: This is, to the authors' knowledge, the first verification that the small-flux Hill–Norbury family is nowhere isolated from the collection of smooth vortex rings.

Abstract: An investigation of local aspects of the space of axisymmetric traveling-wave solutions to the three-dimensional Euler equations near Hill's zero-flux spherical vortex and Norbury's small-flux vortex rings is the endeavor undertaken herein. The aforementioned family consists of idealized, swirl-free, and nonsmooth objects, their azimuthal relative vorticity being prescribed by a scaled characteristic function of the compact vortex core. We confront the desingularization question, both within the swirl-free solution subspace and upon admitting swirl, and prove that each member of the Hill--Norbury family of sufficiently small flux is the limit of traveling-wave solutions to the Euler equations that are smooth to infinite order and have compact toroidal vorticity support. In the latter swirling regime, however, a curious flexibility is revealed: total vorticity may diverge along sequences converging in a natural desingularization topology. Our construction, founded upon a five-dimensional reformulation of the Stokes stream function, is an amalgamation of delicate thin-domain analysis, singular-parameter Newton-type nonlinear inversion, and topological fixed-point arguments. To the best of the authors' knowledge, this constitutes the first verification that the small-flux Hill--Norbury family is nowhere isolated from the collection of smooth vortex rings.

</details>


### [45] [Sobolev Inequalities and the Existence of Solutions to Degenerate $p$-Poisson Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22602)
*David Cruz-Uribe,Feyza Dal,Scott Rodney*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes an equivalence between a gainless Sobolev inequality (for compactly supported smooth functions) and the existence of a degenerate weak solution to a Dirichlet problem for the p-Laplacian with a zero-order term, and proves uniqueness of solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to connect a functional-analytic inequality (Sobolev inequality without gain) with the solvability of a degenerate nonlinear Dirichlet problem, providing a two-way characterization and uniqueness of solutions.

Method: Use the Sobolev inequality to prove existence of a degenerate weak solution (u,∇u) in QH^{1,p}_0(v,Ω) to the p-Laplacian equation with zero-order term F|u|^{p-2}u and forcing terms f, g; conversely, use existence of such a solution to derive the Sobolev inequality. Uniqueness is shown separately.

Result: The main result is an equivalence: the gainless Sobolev inequality holds if and only if the degenerate Dirichlet problem admits a weak solution. Solutions are also shown to be unique.

Conclusion: The gainless Sobolev inequality and the existence of weak solutions to the stated degenerate p-Laplacian Dirichlet problem are equivalent, and the solutions are unique, linking the inequality to the solvability of the nonlinear problem.

Abstract: In this paper we study an equivalence between the existence of a Sobolev inequality without gain, \[\|\varphi\|_{L^p(v,Ω)} \leq S(p,1) \| \sqrt{Q}\nabla \varphi\|_{L^p(Ω)},\] that holds for smooth functions of compact support and the existence of a degenerate weak solution $(u,\nabla u)\in QH^{1,p}_0(v,Ω)$ to a Dirichlet problem for the $p$-Laplacian with a zero order term:
  \begin{equation*}
  -v^{-1}\text{Div}(|\sqrt{Q}\nabla u|^{p-2}Q\nabla u)+F|u|^{p-2}u = |f|^{p-2}f - v^{-1}\text{Div}(v|g|^{p-2}g\mathbf{t}),\; x \in Ω, \quad \text{and} \quad
  u =0, \; x \in \partial Ω, \end{equation*} More precisely, we use the Sobolev inequality to prove the existence of a degenerate weak solution to this equation and then use the existence of such a solution to produce a Sobolev inequality. Moreover, we show that solutions are unique.

</details>


### [46] [Sharp $L^2$-stability and gradient stability hierarchies of the $L^2$-Poincaré inequalities on Euclidean balls and Gaussian Poincaré inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22626)
*Nguyen Lam,Guozhen Lu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Sharp L^2-Poincaré inequalities on Euclidean balls and the Gaussian measure are studied with a focus on stability. Using spectral decomposition, optimal stability estimates and their iterated hierarchy are established, with explicit constants and equality cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp Poincaré constants are first nonzero eigenvalues with finite-dimensional optimizer spaces. The paper aims to extend this to sharp stability estimates and to understand the full hierarchical structure of the Poincaré deficit.

Method: Spectral decomposition of the associated self-adjoint operators. Each stability inequality generates increasing spaces of optimizers, and the process is iterated indefinitely. Spectral gaps and reciprocal-spectrum gaps serve as coefficients.

Result: The Poincaré deficit equals an infinite sum of L^2-distances (and gradient distances) to increasing optimizer spaces, with coefficients μ_j-μ_{j-1} and μ_1(μ_{j-1}^{-1}-μ_j^{-1}). On the Euclidean ball, constants come from Bessel roots; first two spectral levels are degree one and two Neumann modes (all dimensions), third level is the first radial mode for N=2,3 and degree three modes for N≥4. In the Gaussian case all constants are rational and the L^2-sum has all coefficients equal to 1, which characterizes the arithmetic spectrum.

Conclusion: Sharp stability inequalities for the Poincaré inequality admit a complete iterative spectral hierarchy, with explicit optimal constants and fully characterized equality cases in both the Euclidean ball and Gaussian settings.

Abstract: We study the sharp $L^2$-Poincaré inequalities on Euclidean balls and with respect to the Gaussian measure, and we focus on their stability. In both settings, the sharp constant is the first nonzero eigenvalue of a self-adjoint operator and the set of optimizers is a finite dimensional linear space. Using the spectral decomposition, we establish sharp $L^2$-stability estimates and sharp gradient stability estimates, together with the stability of the stability inequalities in both norms, with explicit optimal constants and with the complete characterization of the equality cases. We then show that this process can be continued. The Poincaré deficit is equal to an infinite sum of $L^2$-distances to the increasing spaces of optimizers of the successive stability inequalities. It is also equal to an infinite sum of $L^2$-gradient distances to the same spaces. The coefficients of the first sum are the spectral gaps $μ_j-μ_{j-1}$, and the coefficients of the second sum are $μ_1$ times the gaps of the reciprocal spectrum, $μ_1\left(μ_{j-1}^{-1}-μ_j^{-1}\right)$. On the Euclidean ball, all the constants are expressed through the roots of Bessel functions, and we prove that the first two spectral levels are given by the degree one and the degree two Neumann modes in all dimensions, while the third level is given by the first radial mode when $N=2,3$ and by the degree three modes when $N\geq4$. In the Gaussian case, all the constants are rational numbers and the $L^2$-sum has all coefficients equal to $1$; we prove that this property characterizes the arithmetic spectrum.

</details>


### [47] [Global existence and uniqueness for the planar four-velocity Broadwell model with arbitrary $C^{1}$ bounded initial and boundary data in a rectangular domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22736)
*Koudzo Togbévi Selom Sobah,Amah Séna D'Almeida*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence of classical solutions for the planar four-velocity Broadwell model in a multidimensional setting, removing the small-data size restriction previously required.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work established uniqueness and existence only under sufficiently small, bounded, C^1-regular initial/boundary data. This paper aims to completely lift that size restriction.

Method: Extends the previously developed fixed-point framework, showing the methodology remains effective for arbitrary initial and boundary data.

Result: In the generalized setting, both solutions and their first-order partial derivatives are well-defined and exist globally for all times.

Conclusion: The fixed-point approach generalizes beyond small data, yielding global classical solutions for arbitrary initial and boundary data in the planar four-velocity Broadwell model.

Abstract: This paper establishes the global existence of classical solutions to the initial-boundary value problem for the planar four-velocity Broadwell model in a multidimensional setting. While uniqueness was already established in our previous work (which addressed existence under the assumption of sufficiently small, bounded, and $C^{1}$-regular initial and boundary data), the objective of the present study is to completely lift this size restriction. By extending the previously developed fixed-point framework, we prove that the methodology remains highly effective for arbitrary initial and boundary data. Within this generalized setting, both the solutions and their first-order partial derivatives are shown to be well-defined and to exist globally for all times.

</details>


### [48] [Incompressible Navier-Stokes Limit of the Relativistic Boltzmann Equation with the Optimal Convergence Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22738)
*Yanchao Li,Mingying Zhong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes the diffusion limit of the relativistic Boltzmann equation (RBE) near a global relativistic Maxwellian, showing convergence to the incompressible Navier-Stokes system with the first optimal convergence rate and a precise initial-layer estimate, plus a global strong solution with a light-speed-independent rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work lacked an optimal convergence rate and a precise description of the initial layer in the diffusion limit of the RBE to incompressible Navier-Stokes. This paper aims to fill that gap and also establish well-posedness of global strong solutions.

Method: Spectral analysis of the linear collision operator L, combined with Duhamel's principle, applied to classical solutions with initial data near a global relativistic Maxwellian.

Result: Convergence of the classical RBE solution to the incompressible Navier-Stokes solution is proven, with the first optimal convergence rate and a precise initial-layer estimate. Also obtained: existence and uniqueness of a global strong solution and a convergence rate independent of the speed of light c.

Conclusion: The RBE rigorously converges to incompressible Navier-Stokes with optimal rates and well-characterized initial layer; global strong solutions exist uniquely with rates uniform in c.

Abstract: We study the diffusion limit of the classical solution to the relativistic Boltzmann equation with the initial data near a global relativistic Maxwellian. By using spectral analysis, we establish the convergence of the classical solution to the relativistic Boltzmann equation to that of the incompressible Navier-Stokes system, and give for the first time the optimal convergence rate and a precise estimate of the initial layer. Moreover, through a refined analysis of the linear collision operator $L$, combined with Duhamel's principle, we obtain the existence and uniqueness of a global strong solution to the relativistic Boltzmann equation, as well as a convergence rate independent of the speed of light $\mathbf{c}.$

</details>


### [49] [On the stability of the solutions to compressible Navier-Stokes equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22749)
*Qiaojie Dong,Min Li,Yatao Li,Minghua Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves continuous dependence of solutions to barotropic compressible Navier-Stokes equations in critical Besov spaces, closing a gap by showing the Eulerian flow map is continuous without loss of regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work established Lipschitz continuity in Lagrangian coordinates, but this doesn't imply continuous dependence in Eulerian coordinates due to low regularity of critical initial data. Only for p<d was continuity known, and only in lower-regularity spaces with a loss of one derivative.

Method: Uses the Lagrangian approach of Danchin (2014), analyzing the flow map (a_0,u_0) to (a,u) in critical Besov spaces, proving continuity rather than Lipschitz continuity.

Result: For 1<p<2d, the flow map (a_0,u_0)→(a,u) is continuous from the critical Besov space product into C([0,T];B^{d/p}_{p,1})×E_p(T) in Eulerian coordinates, with no loss of regularity.

Conclusion: Combined with the known existence and uniqueness theory, this completes Hadamard well-posedness in the critical spaces.

Abstract: This paper investigates the continuous dependence (stability) of solutions to the barotropic compressible Navier--Stokes equations in critical Besov spaces. Using the Lagrangian approach developed in \cite{Danchin2014}, it was shown that, for \(1<p<2d\), the flow map $(a_0,u_0)\mapsto (\bar a,\bar u)=(a\circ X,u\circ X)$ is Lipschitz continuous from $\dot B_{p,1}^{d/p}\times \dot B_{p,1}^{d/p-1}$ into $\mathcal{C}([0,T];\dot B_{p,1}^{d/p})\times E_p(T)$. However, this result does not directly imply the corresponding continuous dependence in the original Eulerian coordinates because of the low regularity of the critical initial data. Previously, only for $p<d$, continuous dependence was known only in certain lower-regularity spaces with a loss of one derivative relative to the natural solution spaces arising essentially as a by-product of the uniqueness argument. We close this gap and prove that for $1<p<2d$ the flow map $(a_0,u_0)\mapsto(a,u)$ is continuous (not Lipschitz continuous) from $\dot B_{p,1}^{d/p}\times\dot B_{p,1}^{d/p-1}$ to $\mathcal{C}([0,T];\dot B_{p,1}^{d/p})\times E_p(T)$ in Eulerian coordinates without any loss of regularity, which together with the known existence and uniqueness theory \cite{Danchin2014} completes Hadamard well-posedness in the critical spaces.

</details>


### [50] [A fractional Brezis-Nirenberg problem with multipolar Hardy potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22877)
*Debangana Mukherjee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Fractional Brezis-Nirenberg problem with multiple Hardy potentials: existence of positive least-energy solutions for a range of λ including 0 and negative values, plus a direct compactness estimate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish existence of positive least-energy solutions for a fractional Brezis-Nirenberg problem with finitely many Hardy potentials, extending existence to λ=0 and negative λ via pole interactions.

Method: Assumes sum of masses < Λ_{N,s}, N>4s, μ_max < overline μ_{N,s}. Analyzes pole interaction via B_j(λ,a,μ), yielding threshold λ_geo ≤ 0. Proves attainability of critical Rayleigh quotient; uses direct compactness estimate for weakly vanishing sequences by localizing the Gagliardo seminorm, avoiding profile decomposition and Caffarelli-Silvestre extension.

Result: Critical Rayleigh quotient is attained for λ_geo < λ < λ_1(μ,a), so a positive least-energy solution exists. When at least two masses are positive, λ_geo < 0, covering λ=0 and a nonempty interval of negative λ.

Conclusion: Interaction between poles enables existence beyond the classical range, including λ=0 and negative values; a new direct compactness argument provides a technically simpler proof.

Abstract: We study a fractional Brezis--Nirenberg problem on a bounded domain with finitely many Hardy potentials centered at distinct points $a_1,\dots,a_k$, with masses $μ_1,\dots,μ_k\geq 0$. We assume $\sum_{i=1}^kμ_i<Λ_{N,s}$ and consider $N>4s$ and $0<μ_{\max}<\overlineμ_{N,s}$. The interaction between the poles allows existence for $λ=0$ and for some negative values of $λ$. At a pole $a_j$, this interaction is described by $B_j(λ,\mathbf a,\boldsymbolμ)=λ+\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{μ_i}{|a_i-a_j|^{2s}}$. This leads to a threshold $λ_{\mathrm{geo}}\leq 0$. We prove that the critical Rayleigh quotient is attained for $λ_{\mathrm{geo}}<λ<λ_1(\boldsymbolμ,\mathbf a)$, and hence the problem admits a positive least-energy solution. If at least two masses are positive, then $λ_{\mathrm{geo}}<0$, so the result includes $λ=0$ and a nonempty interval of negative values of $λ$. We also prove a direct compactness estimate for weakly vanishing sequences by localizing the Gagliardo seminorm. The argument avoids both profile decomposition and the Caffarelli-Silvestre extension.

</details>


### [51] [Finite time singularity formation for a nonlocal Boussinesq system arising in cold-plasma dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22911)
*Diego Alonso-Orán,Rafael Granero-Belinchón*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves finite-time blow-up for a nonlocal Boussinesq system from cold-plasma dynamics, giving an explicit lifespan upper bound and showing the velocity gradient becomes unbounded.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding singularity formation in nonlocal Boussinesq systems is important for cold-plasma dynamics, yet blow-up conditions for such systems remain to be characterized.

Method: Analysis of smooth, symmetric initial data with strictly positive total density and a sufficiently negative velocity slope at the origin.

Result: Such initial data lead to finite-time blow-up; an explicit upper bound on the lifespan is derived and the velocity gradient is shown to become unbounded.

Conclusion: The nonlocal Boussinesq system exhibits finite-time singularity formation under the stated conditions on density and velocity slope.

Abstract: We study finite-time singularity formation for a nonlocal Boussinesq system arising in cold-plasma dynamics. We show that smooth, symmetric initial data with strictly positive total density and a sufficiently negative velocity slope at the origin lead to finite-time blow-up. More precisely, we give an explicit upper bound for the lifespan and show that the velocity gradient becomes unbounded.

</details>


### [52] [Uniqueness, Nondegeneracy, and Asymptotic Expansions of Ground States for the Dirac-Coulomb System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22953)
*Pan Chen,Qi Guo,Xiaoyu Zeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniqueness (modulo symmetries) of ground states for a Dirac-Coulomb system with a self-consistent Coulomb potential under an L² normalization constraint, for sufficiently large c, and derives their nonrelativistic asymptotic expansions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ground states of nonlinear Dirac-Coulomb systems are physically relevant models (e.g., relativistic self-gravitating or Coulomb-interacting Dirac fields), but uniqueness and linearized-operator structure are subtle due to translation, phase, and spinorial symmetries. Understanding the nonrelativistic limit is also of interest.

Method: Variational/analytic study of the constrained Dirac-Coulomb system with Hartree-type nonlocal interaction (|x|⁻¹ * |u_c|²)u_c and eigenvalue ω_c under ∫|u_c|²=1. Uses spectral analysis and the linearized operator around the ground state, together with asymptotic expansion techniques in the nonrelativistic limit c→∞.

Result: For sufficiently large c, uniqueness of ground states is established modulo translations, phase transformations, and spinorial rotations. The kernel of the linearized operator at the ground state is determined, and asymptotic expansions of the ground state in the nonrelativistic limit regime are obtained.

Conclusion: The Dirac-Coulomb ground state is essentially unique for large c, with a characterized linearization kernel and known nonrelativistic asymptotics, providing a rigorous basis for further stability and dynamical analysis.

Abstract: We study ground states of the following Dirac-Coulomb system $$\mathscr{D}_cu_c-
  \left(\abs{x}^{-1}\ast \abs{u_c}^2\right)u_c=ω_cu_c,$$ under the constraint
  $\norm[L^2]{u_c}^2=1.$For sufficiently large \(c\), we prove uniqueness of ground states modulo translations, phase transformations and spinorial rotations, and determine the kernel of the linearized operator at ground state. We also obtain asymptotic expansions of ground state in the nonrelativistic limit regime.

</details>


### [53] [The Binary-ternary Boltzmann Equation Near Equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22980)
*Xianshen Hu,Linjie Xiong,Zhihong Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global existence near equilibrium for the binary-ternary Boltzmann equation, extending the classical Boltzmann framework to account for multi-particle (three-body) interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Boltzmann equation is inadequate for gases that are no longer sufficiently dilute. Higher-order extensions capturing multi-particle interactions are needed. Although Ampatzoglou and Pavlović rigorously derived the ternary and binary-ternary Boltzmann equations with an explicit ternary collision operator, the global existence near equilibrium for the binary-ternary equation remained open.

Method: The paper studies the binary-ternary Boltzmann equation, which combines binary and ternary collision operators, building on the recent rigorous derivations of these operators for a classical particle system. The authors analyze the well-posedness of this equation near equilibrium.

Result: The paper proves global existence of solutions to the binary-ternary Boltzmann equation near equilibrium, thereby addressing the previously open fundamental question.

Conclusion: The work fills a gap in the theory of higher-order Boltzmann-type equations by establishing global existence near equilibrium for the binary-ternary model, contributing to the understanding of non-dilute gas dynamics with multi-particle interactions.

Abstract: The classical Boltzmann equation, formulated by Boltzmann in 1872, describes the statistical behavior of a dilute gas in a non-equilibrium state. While the global well-posedness of the classical Boltzmann equation has been extensively studied and is relatively well-understood, this model becomes inadequate when the gas is no longer sufficiently dilute. Therefore, it is necessary to investigate higher-order extensions of the Boltzmann equation that account for multi-particle interactions. Recently, Ampatzoglou and Pavlović provided the rigorous derivation of both the ternary [\textit{Comm. Math. Phys.} \textbf{387} (2021), no. 2, 793-863.] and the binary-ternary [\textit{Forum Math. Sigma} \textbf{13} (2025), Paper No. e52, 95 pp.] Boltzmann equations from a classical particle system, yielding an explicit form of the ternary collision operator. However, the global existence near equilibrium for the binary-ternary Boltzmann equation remains an interesting and fundamental question, a gap that this paper seeks to address as its primary contribution.

</details>


### [54] [Stability of strong global and exponential attractors for semilinear beam equations with fractional damping and memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23002)
*Yu-Ying Duan,Ti-Jun Xiao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies stability of strong global and exponential attractors for a semilinear beam equation with memory and fractional damping, proving existence, upper semicontinuity, and continuity in parameters (α, β).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing β=0 results hold in weaker topologies; this work aims to establish parameter-robust attractor properties in a stronger topology for the coupled memory and fractional damping model.

Method: Uses recently developed higher-order regularity results for global attractors, plus analysis of fractional damping (α∈[0,2]) and memory parameter (β∈[0,1]).

Result: Proves existence of a strong global attractor and its upper semicontinuity in (α, β); constructs strong exponential attractors and establishes their continuity in (α, β), with compactness/attraction in a topology stronger than the phase space.

Conclusion: The results generalize and strengthen prior β=0 findings by yielding attractor properties in a stronger topology.

Abstract: This paper investigates the stability of strong global and exponential attractors for a semilinear beam equation with memory and fractional damping, where $α\in[0,2]$ denotes the fractional damping exponent and $β\in[0,1]$ the memory parameter. After showing the existence of a strong global attractor, we prove its upper semicontinuity in the parameter pair $(α,β)$. We then construct a family of strong exponential attractors and establish its continuity in $(α,β)$. Here, ``strong" means that the compactness, attraction, and parameter-robustness properties are established in a topology stronger than that of the phase space. Compared to existing $β= 0$ results, our findings hold in a stronger topology. The analysis draws upon our recent higher-order regularity results for global attractors.

</details>


### [55] [Interior Hessian estimates for k-convex solutions of Hessian quotient equations with 2k>n](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23006)
*Ke Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes interior Hessian estimates for k-convex solutions of the equation σ_k(D²u)/σ_l(D²u)=1 under conditions 2≤l<k<n, k−l∈{1,2}, and 2k>n, using a concavity inequality combined with a pointwise doubling argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interior Hessian estimates are central to the regularity theory of fully nonlinear elliptic PDEs. The quotients of elementary symmetric functions σ_k/σ_l arise in geometric and conformal problems, and extending second-order derivative bounds to this general quotient setting addresses a gap beyond classical cases.

Method: The proof combines a concavity inequality (for the relevant symmetric function quotient) with a pointwise doubling argument to derive the interior Hessian bound for k-convex solutions.

Result: Interior Hessian estimates are obtained for k-convex solutions of σ_k(D²u)/σ_l(D²u)=1 for 2≤l<k<n with k−l∈{1,2} and 2k>n.

Conclusion: The paper yields interior Hessian estimates for a class of quotient equations of elementary symmetric functions, extending regularity results under the stated range of indices.

Abstract: We establish interior Hessian estimates for $k$ convex solutions of $\frac{σ_k(D^2u)}{σ_l(D^2u)}=1$, where $2 \leq l < k < n$, $k-l \in \{1,2\}$, and $2k > n$.The proof combines a concavity inequality with a pointwise doubling argument.

</details>


### [56] [Analysis of Bregman Proximal Point for the Obstacle Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23007)
*Brendan Keith,Haojun Qin,Noe Reyes Rivas*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a Bregman proximal point method for the obstacle problem, establishing well-posedness, strict feasibility, and $H^1$-norm convergence rates governed by the order of sequential strict local minimality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The obstacle problem is a fundamental variational inequality in contact mechanics, optimal design, and mathematical finance, motivating the development of a Bregman-based proximal method with rigorous convergence guarantees.

Method: Each Bregman proximal step regularizes the energy via a Bregman divergence from a Legendre function, yielding a semilinear elliptic subproblem. Convergence is analyzed through a sequential strict local minimality inequality, with the order of minimality determining the rate.

Result: Under an initial guess above the exact solution and a one-sided Bregman growth condition, order $s \geq 2$ minimality gives sublinear $H^1$ rates $O(k^{-\zeta})$, $\zeta\in(1/2,1]$; these are sharp for Shannon and Tsallis entropies. With additional free-boundary/obstacle assumptions, order $s=1$ minimality for Shannon and Spence entropies yields linear convergence $O(\varrho^k)$.

Conclusion: The order of sequential strict local minimality controls the convergence rate of the Bregman proximal point method, ranging from sublinear (sharp for some entropies) to linear convergence under stronger structural assumptions.

Abstract: We study the Bregman proximal point method for the obstacle problem, a fundamental variational inequality arising in contact mechanics, optimal design, and mathematical finance. Each Bregman proximal step regularizes the energy through a Bregman divergence generated by a Legendre function, leading to a semilinear elliptic subproblem. We first establish a well-posedness and strict feasibility theory for these subproblems. We then analyze the convergence of the resulting iteration in the $H^1$-norm. Our convergence analysis is based on a sequential strict local minimality inequality. We show abstractly how the order of minimality determines the convergence rate. Assuming that the initial guess lies above the exact solution and a one-sided Bregman growth condition holds, we establish sequential strict local minimality of order $s \geq 2$ and derive sublinear convergence rates in the $H^1$-norm of the form $O(k^{-ζ})$, where $ζ\in(1/2,1]$ depends on the choice of Legendre function. For the Shannon and Tsallis entropies, we show that these rates are sharp in a uniform worst-case sense. Under additional assumptions on the free boundary and the obstacle, we establish sequential strict local minimality of order $s = 1$ with the Shannon and Spence entropies. This yields linear convergence of the form $O(\varrho^k)$ for some $\varrho\in(0,1)$.

</details>


### [57] [Free Boundary Propagation of a Thin-Film Equation with Nonlinear Diffusion: Scale-Consistent Methods for Optimal Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23022)
*Joshua Utley*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies free boundary propagation for a nonlinear degenerate parabolic equation mixing fourth- and second-order nonlinearities, deriving asymptotically optimal propagation rate bounds and analyzing the shift in dominant operator over time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The problem models viscous thin-film flow under weak slippage with an added singular disjoining pressure from intermolecular forces. Understanding how competing fourth- and second-order nonlinearities govern free boundary motion/time scales was previously unclear.

Method: Treat individual scaling behaviors of the two nonlinearities to derive asymptotically optimal upper and lower bounds on the forward support propagation rate.

Result: The fourth-order operator dominates propagation on short time scales, while the second-order operator controls long-term propagation. The time of this trade-off is estimated, and sufficient conditions for non-immediate free boundary advance in a given direction are given.

Conclusion: The competition between second- and fourth-order operators strongly shapes free boundary dynamics, yielding a detailed picture of solution behavior across time regimes.

Abstract: We investigate the free boundary propagation of a non-linear degenerate parabolic problem with both fourth and second order non-linearities, which may be used to model viscous thin-film flow in the regime of weak slippage with an additional contributions from a singular disjoining pressure brought forth by inter-molecular forces. By carefully treating the individual scaling behaviors of the non-linearities, we obtain asymptotically optimal upper and lower bounds on the rate of forward support propagation. These rates show that the fourth-order operator dominates the propagation rate on short time scales, but the long-term rate of propagation is controlled by the second-order operator. We estimate further the time at which this trade-off in propagation rate occurs. Lastly, we examine sufficient conditions to ensure that the free boundary does not immediately advance in a given direction, leading to a detailed picture of how the competition between the second/fourth-order operators influence the free boundary dynamics of solutions.

</details>


### [58] [Wave propagation of a non-conservative compressible two-fluid model with different pressures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23095)
*Zhigang Wu,Yinghui Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies wave propagation for a non-conservative compressible two-fluid model with unequal pressures in R3, revealing new diffusion-wave behavior distinct from classical Navier-Stokes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The model is novel and differs fundamentally from classical single-phase compressible Navier-Stokes equations (Liu-Wang). Prior results apply only to conservative or partially conservative systems with specific Green's function cancellations, leaving a significant gap for non-conservative systems with unequal pressures.

Method: Dedicated nonlinear coupling analysis addressing the non-conservative nature and the coexistence of two distinct-speed Huygens waves.

Result: For equal pressure, the stationary diffusion wave comprises a single Huygens wave, analogous to classical Navier-Stokes. Under unequal pressure, the stationary diffusion wave consists of two Huygens waves propagating at different speeds—a novel phenomenon absent in the classical Navier-Stokes context.

Conclusion: The study reveals fundamentally different physical behaviors of the non-conservative two-phase model under equal versus unequal interfacial pressures, filling a gap left by prior work.
