Abstract: This paper investigates the wave propagation for a non-conservative compressible two-fluid model with unequal pressures in R3. This model is novel and fundamentally distinct from the classical single-phase compressible Navier-Stokes equations proposed by Liu-Wang [28]. This work fills a significant gap, as prior results largely apply only to conservative or partially conservative systems with specific Green's function cancellations. The non-conservative nature and the coexistence of two distinct-speed Huygens waves demand dedicated nonlinear coupling analysis and add further complexity. Our study reveals fundamentally different physical behaviors of this non-conservative two-phase model under equal and unequal interfacial pressures. For equal pressure, the model admits a stationary diffusion wave comprising a single Huygens wave, analogous to classical Navier-Stokes equations. In sharp contrast, under unequal pressure, the stationary diffusion wave consists of two Huygens waves propagating at different speeds--a novel phenomenon absent in the classical Navier-Stokes context.

</details>


### [59] [Local Bifurcations from Single-Mode Traveling Waves in the Filamentation Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23145)
*Yuri Cacchiò*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Local bifurcations from the single-mode traveling wave family of the filamentation equation on the torus are studied via Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction, revealing analytic branches at critical values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the bifurcation structure of the filamentation equation's single-mode traveling wave family on the torus, particularly the role of Fourier null structure in removing derivative loss.

Method: Works in real positive-frequency Sobolev space $X^s$; exploits a Fourier null structure making the traveling wave map real analytic for $s>3/2$. Linearization splits into coupled two-mode blocks, a carrier block, and a diagonal high-frequency tail. Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction is applied at critical values.

Result: For negative temporal frequency, critical values arise from finite coupled blocks and the tail. Each non-resonant finite-block critical value yields a locally unique real-analytic branch with vanishing linear correction. Each finite tail critical value gives a simple local branch. Values accumulate where linearization is non-Fredholm, yet this accumulation point is still a bifurcation point topologically. For $\\sigma=1$, a symmetry yields an exact two-mode vertical branch, including the non-Fredholm case $k=2$.

Conclusion: Bifurcation branches are constructed and characterized, with accumulation at a non-Fredholm parameter that remains a topological bifurcation point; symmetry produces an explicit two-mode branch for $\\sigma=1$.

Abstract: We study local bifurcations from the single-mode traveling wave family of the filamentation equation on the torus, within the real positive-frequency Sobolev space $X^s$. A Fourier null structure removes the apparent derivative loss and makes the traveling wave map real analytic for $s>3/2$. Its linearization splits into finitely many coupled two-mode blocks, a carrier block, and a diagonal high-frequency tail. For negative temporal frequency, we determine the critical values generated by both the finite coupled blocks and the tail. At every non resonant finite-block critical value, Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction yields a locally unique real-analytic branch, with vanishing linear correction to the bifurcation parameter. Moreover, each finite tail critical value produces a simple local branch. These values accumulate at a parameter where the linearization ceases to be Fredholm. The accumulation point is nevertheless a bifurcation point in the usual topological sense. For $σ=1$, a symmetry in the first Fourier mode produces an exact two-mode vertical branch, including the exceptional non-Fredholm case $k=2$.

</details>


### [60] [A fully nonlinear two-phase Alt-Phillips problem with gradient nonlinearities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23216)
*Junior da Silva Bessa,João Vitor da Silva,Yuwei Hu,Mayra Soares*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a two-phase free boundary problem of Alt-Phillips type governed by fully nonlinear elliptic equations with mixed linear/nonlinear gradient terms and two-phase power nonlinearities, establishing growth estimates at singular nodal points, quantitative non-degeneracy, and a Liouville-type theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Two-phase free boundary problems with fully nonlinear operators and lower-order (gradient) ingredients lack a unified analytical framework. The authors aim to extend and unify earlier Alt-Phillips-type results, including cases with nonlinear drift and absorption effects relevant to phase transition and reaction-diffusion models.

Method: They analyze F(x,D²u)+H(x,Du)=g(x)[(u⁺)^m−(u⁻)^m] in B₁, with H combining a linear drift ⟨B(x),ξ⟩ and a nonlinear term ρ(x)|ξ|^σ (0<σ≤2, σ≠1), and 0<m<1. The approach relies on improved growth estimates, a fine blow-up argument, stability, and a Liouville-type theorem.

Result: The authors obtain improved growth estimates at higher-order singular nodal points (where both phases meet with critical degeneracy), quantitative non-degeneracy showing solutions detach from zero at a controlled rate on each phase, and a Liouville-type theorem for global solutions in this class.

Conclusion: The framework extends and unifies several previously known scenarios, remaining new even with gradient-dependent nonlinearities of order σ≠1, and covers models with nonlinear drift and absorption in phase transition and reaction-diffusion phenomena.

Abstract: In this manuscript, we analyze a two-phase free boundary problem of Alt-Phillips-type driven by fully nonlinear elliptic equations with lower-order ingredients. More precisely, we consider equations of the type \[
  F(x,D^{2}u)+\mathscr{H}(x,Du)=\mathscr{F}(x,u^{+},u^{-}) \quad \text{in } B_1 \subset \mathbb{R}^{n}, \] where the Hamiltonian term exhibits mixed linear and nonlinear gradient dependence, namely \[ \mathscr{H}(x,ξ):= \langle \mathcal{B}(x),ξ\rangle+\varrho(x)|ξ|^σ, \quad 0< σ\leq2, \,\,\, σ\neq 1, \] and the reaction is governed by a two-phase power-type nonlinearity of semilinear-type \[ \mathscr{F}(x,u^{+},u^{-}):=\mathfrak{g}(x)\big[(u^{+})^{m}-(u^{-})^{m}\big] \quad \text{for} \quad 0<m<1. \] Under suitable structural assumptions on the coefficients and the operator $F$, we develop a robust analytical framework to capture the fine properties of solutions near the free boundary. In particular, we establish improved growth estimates at higher-order singular nodal points, points where both phases meet and the solution exhibits critical degeneracy. The results are based upon a fine blow-up argument, stability and a Liouville-type theorem. We further prove quantitative non-degeneracy results, ensuring that solutions detach from zero at a controlled rate on each phase. As a consequence of our analysis, we derive a Liouville-type theorem for global solutions within this class. Our results extend and unify several previously known scenarios, and remain new even in the presence of gradient-dependent nonlinearities of order ($σ\neq 1$), thereby encompassing models with nonlinear drift and absorption effects arising in phase transition and reaction-diffusion phenomena.

</details>


### [61] [On the potential lack of response in a model of second-harmonic generation. A computer-assisted proof](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23217)
*Miguel Ayala,Dominic Blanco,Fioralba Cakoni,Narek Hovsepyan,Michael S. Vogelius*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper rigorously proves the existence of generalized transmission eigenvalues in 1D nonlinear optics (high-order harmonic generation), using a Newton-Kantorovich framework with interval arithmetic, confirming a prior conjecture and showing eigenfunction blow-up as frequency tends to zero.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: These eigenvalues correspond to probing frequencies for which an incident wave produces a second-harmonic field that does not persist outside the nonlinear medium's support, making the medium's nonlinear properties invisible to an external observer. The existence of such eigenvalues was conjectured in Cakoni, Hovsepyan, Lassas, and Vogelius (SIAM J. Math. Anal. 57, 2025), motivating a rigorous proof.

Method: The problem is reduced to a coupled system of nonlinear homogeneous ODEs with nonstandard boundary conditions in 1D. The proof combines analytical arguments with validated numerics, using a Newton-Kantorovich framework and interval arithmetic to rigorously control approximation errors. The implementation is available in the GitHub repository TransmissionEigenvalues.jl by Dominic Blanco (2026).

Result: The paper establishes that, in the low-frequency regime, generalized transmission eigenvalues exist, and their associated eigenfunctions exhibit blow-up behavior as the frequency tends to zero. This is a rigorous, computer-assisted confirmation of earlier numerical evidence.

Conclusion: The conjecture on generalized transmission eigenvalues is confirmed via computer-assisted proof, providing a rigorous foundation for these eigenvalue phenomena and their implications for undetectable nonlinear media.

Abstract: This paper provides a rigorous computer-assisted proof of the existence of generalized transmission eigenvalues arising in nonlinear optics in the context of high-order harmonic generation, a result conjectured in Cakoni, Hovsepyan, Lassas, and Vogelius, SIAM J. Math. Anal. 57 (2025), 1370-1405. The analysis is carried out for a one-dimensional nonlinear medium, where the problem reduces to a coupled system of nonlinear homogeneous ordinary differential equations subject to nonstandard boundary conditions. These eigenvalues correspond to probing frequencies $ω$ for which there exists a nontrivial incident $ω$-wave such that the second-harmonic field generated does not persist outside the compact support of the nonlinear medium, thereby rendering its nonlinear properties undetectable to an external observer. Building on earlier numerical evidence, we establish that, in the low-frequency regime, there exist generalized transmission eigenvalues whose associated eigenfunctions exhibit blow-up behavior as the frequency tends to zero. The proof combines analytical arguments with validated numerics, employing a Newton-Kantorovich framework together with interval arithmetic to rigorously control approximation errors. The algorithmic implementation underlying the computer-assisted proof is made available in the GitHub repository TransmissionEigenvalues.jl by Dominic Blanco (2026).

</details>


### [62] [Sharp $L^2$ estimates for wave equations with non-integrable speeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23230)
*Halit Sevki Aslan,Wenhui Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes long-time asymptotics of the displacement for wave equations with non-integrable (growing) speeds, deriving matching upper and lower bounds under suitable conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wave equations with non-integrable propagation speeds exhibit unusual large-time behavior, but sharp estimates for the displacement (not just the solution) were lacking, especially without explicit Fourier multipliers.

Method: Assumes zero initial displacement and initial velocities in L^2 ∩ L^1. Derives an L^2 upper bound with rate governed by the speed and its time integral. Under stronger speed assumptions and a non-trivial mean condition on the initial velocity, proves a matching lower bound without an explicit Fourier multiplier formula.

Result: Upper and lower bounds of the same order are obtained; the estimates apply to polynomial, logarithmically modified, log-periodically oscillating, and exponential-type speeds.

Conclusion: The large-time displacement behavior is fully characterized (up to constants) for broad classes of non-integrable speeds, with a method avoiding explicit Fourier multipliers.

Abstract: We study the large-time behavior of the displacement for wave equations with non-integrable speeds and zero initial displacement. For initial velocities in $L^2\cap L^1$, we derive an upper bound estimate in the $L^2$ norm, with the rate determined by the propagation speed and its time integral. Under stronger assumptions on the propagation speed and the non-trivial mean condition on the initial velocity, we derive a lower bound estimate of the same order without using an explicit formula for the Fourier multiplier. The estimates apply to polynomial, logarithmically modified, log-periodically oscillating, and exponential-type speeds.

</details>


### [63] [Scaling limits for Moran processes on metric strategy spaces: an Eulerian derivation of pure replicator and Fleming--Viot measure-valued PDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23235)
*Stefano Almi,Riccardo Durastanti,Marco Morandotti,Gianluca Orlando,Francesco Solombrino*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper derives continuum limits of a discrete-time multi-strategy Moran process over a general (possibly infinite) metric strategy space, showing that the limiting PDE depends on scaling relations among population size, mutation rate, and selection intensity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how large-population limits of evolutionary dynamics with many strategies depend on relative scaling of population size, mutation, and selection, extending classical finite-strategy results to general metric strategy spaces.

Method: Reinterpret the discrete Moran process in Eulerian density coordinates, construct interpolating curves satisfying an approximate PDE, and prove convergence using a compactness argument in a topology on probability measures over probabilities on $\mathcal{V}$.

Result: The limit is governed either by a deterministic replicator-type continuity equation or by a diffusion-enhanced PDE; the latter recovers the Fleming–Viot operator and the Kimura equation as special cases.

Conclusion: The large-population behavior of the multi-strategy Moran process is characterized by distinct scaling regimes, unifying deterministic replicator dynamics and diffusion-type (Fleming–Viot/Kimura) limits within a single framework.

Abstract: We study the large-population limit of a discrete-time Moran process featuring multiple strategies, drawn from a possibly infinite strategy space $\mathcal{V}$ (a metric space), under both weak and strong selection. In the Eulerian density formulation, the limiting dynamics depend critically on the relative scaling of population size, mutation rate, and selection intensity. Depending on these scalings, the limit behavior is governed either by a purely deterministic replicator-type continuity equation or by a diffusion-enhanced PDE. In the latter case, the diffusion operator recovers the classical Fleming--Viot operator and the Kimura equation as special instances. Methodologically, we derive the limit by reinterpreting the discrete process in Eulerian coordinates, constructing interpolating curves that satisfy an approximate PDE, and establishing convergence via a compactness argument in a suitable topology on the space of probability measures over probabilities over $\mathcal{V}$.

</details>


### [64] [Existence and non-existence of extremal functions for inequalities of Bliss-Moser type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23251)
*Yanyan Guo,Huxiao Luo,Bernhard Ruf*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Concentration-compactness theory for a 1D singular Moser-type functional with logarithmic weight, resolving an open problem and characterizing critical Bliss-Moser behavior for all c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolves open problem from do Ó–Ruf–Ubilla (CVPDE 2023); understands how linear perturbations affect compactness, concentration, and attainability at critical level.

Method: Concentration-compactness analysis of singular Moser-type functional on E={u∈H¹: u(0)=0, ∫|u'|²=1} with logarithmic weight; study subcritical range γ<1 and critical Bliss-Moser inequality with perturbation cs.

Result: Proves compactness for all normalized sequences when γ<1. For critical case, determines sharp concentration level sup limsup I^c(v_j)=1+e² for all c; gives explicit sufficient conditions for attainability (c>c*) and non-attainability (c<-c_*).

Conclusion: Linear perturbations govern transition between compactness, concentration, and attainability at the critical level.

Abstract: We establish a concentration--compactness theory for a one-dimensional singular Moser-type functional with logarithmic weight function $$\sup\limits_{v\in E }\int_0^1 e^{\left(\log\frac{e}{s}+γ\log\log\frac{e}{s}\right)\frac{v^2(s)}{s}}ds<\infty,~γ\in[0,1],$$ where $$E:= \left\{u \in H^{1}(0, 1): u(0) = 0, \int_0^1 |u'|^2dx=1\right\}.$$ In particular, we prove compactness for all normalized sequences in the subcritical range $γ<1$, thereby resolving an open problem proposed in [J. M. do Ó, B. Ruf and P. Ubilla, CVPDE 2023]. We also study the critical Bliss-Moser inequality: \begin{equation}\nonumber \sup\limits_{v\in E }I^c(v)< +\infty,\quad I^c(v)=\int_0^1 e^{\left(\log\frac{e}{s}+\log\log\frac{e}{s}+cs\right)\frac{v^2(s)}{s}}ds. \end{equation} For any $c\in(-\infty,+\infty)$, we determine the sharp concentration level $$\sup_{v_j\in E,v_j~\text{is~NCS}}\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty} I^c(v_j)=1+e^2,$$ and obtain explicit sufficient conditions for attainability under $c>c^*$ and non-attainability under $c<-c_*$. This results describe how linear perturbations govern the transition between compactness, concentration, and attainability at the critical level.

</details>


### [65] [Mean field games with Hormander diffusions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23283)
*Yiming Jiang,Jingchuang Ren,Yawei Wei,Jie Xue*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes well-posedness of degenerate mean field game systems with Hormander diffusion in intrinsic Holder spaces, developing a global regularity theory for degenerate parabolic equations on Hormander-induced tori.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Agents can only move along admissible directions (Hormander diffusion), and the anisotropic geometry causes non-commutativity and inhomogeneity of underlying vector fields, which standard methods cannot handle.

Method: A priori Schauder estimates for general Hormander degenerate parabolic equations via Campanato spaces, providing an alternative to the classical fundamental solution framework.

Result: Well-posedness of the MFG systems in intrinsic Holder spaces, describing Nash equilibria for a differential game with infinitely many small players; first global regularity theory for degenerate parabolic equations on Hormander vector field tori without group structure.

Conclusion: The Campanato space approach successfully overcomes the anisotropic geometry difficulties, yielding a general global regularity theory and well-posedness of degenerate MFG systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we study a class of degenerate mean field games (MFG, for short) systems with Hormander diffusion, in which the typical agent can move only along admissible direction. We establish the well-posedness of the MFG systems in intrinsic Holder spaces, which describes the Nash equilibria for a differential game with infinitely many small players. The analysis builds upon degenerate parabolic equations defined on the tours induced by Hormander vector fields without any group structure, for which we develop a global regularity theory for the first time in this general setting. A central difficulty arises from the anisotropic geometry of the state space, which induces non-commutativity and inhomogeneity of the underlying vector fields. Instead of the classical fundamental solution framework, we present an alternative method to derive a priori Schauder estimates for general Hormander degenerate parabolic equations via Campanato spaces.

</details>


### [66] [Global flows for the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation without decay assumptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23324)
*Shinichi Kotani,Yiqian Wang,Jiahao Xu,Shuo Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs global spectral group actions for defocusing and focusing nonlinear Schrödinger hierarchies, proving global well-posedness on the Banach space M^5_{∞,1}(R) for both signs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing integrable-systems frameworks (Sato–Segal–Wilson, Kotani) for NLS were limited to scalar problems and required decay, smallness, periodicity, or arithmetic conditions; extending them to broader initial-data classes without such restrictions is the goal.

Method: Global spectral group actions are built via matrix Dirac systems, extending Sato–Segal–Wilson and Kotani frameworks; a homogeneous tau-function identity yields focusing positivity. For N≥2 the quadratic flow gives classical cubic NLS solutions. The Banach-space passage uses near-real-axis Weyl asymptotics and continuity of normalized Toeplitz operators on compact sets, deriving uniform spatial bounds from five bounded derivatives.

Result: Initial-data classes include W^{2N+1,∞}(R); global well-posedness on M^5_{∞,1}(R) for both signs, with finite-time bounds and locally Lipschitz dependence on norm balls. In the defocusing case the flow extends to M^5_{∞,1}(R)+H^1(R).

Conclusion: The construction removes decay, smallness, periodicity, arithmetic, and spectral-background requirements. Consequences include quantitative approximation, preservation of spatial Bohr almost periodicity and frequency module, conjugacy of translation hulls, and preservation of the complex Dirac spectrum and spatial transfer growth rates.

Abstract: For each positive integer $N$, we construct global spectral group actions for the defocusing and focusing nonlinear Schrödinger hierarchies on initial-data classes containing $W^{2N+1,\infty}(\mathbb R)$. For $N\ge2$, the quadratic flow gives global classical cubic NLS solutions. The construction extends the Sato--Segal--Wilson and Kotani frameworks to matrix Dirac systems; a homogeneous tau-function identity supplies the focusing positivity. No decay, smallness, periodicity, arithmetic condition, or prescribed spectral background is required for the initial-data inclusion. As a principal application, we prove global well-posedness on $M_{\infty,1}^{5}(\mathbb R)$ for both signs, with finite-time bounds and locally Lipschitz dependence on initial-data norm balls. The passage to this Banach-space flow uses near-real-axis Weyl asymptotics and continuity of normalized Toeplitz operators on compact initial-data sets to obtain uniform spatial bounds from five bounded derivatives. In the defocusing case, the flow extends to $M_{\infty,1}^{5}(\mathbb R)+H^1(\mathbb R)$. Further consequences include quantitative approximation, preservation of spatial Bohr almost periodicity and its frequency module, conjugacy of spatial translation hulls, and preservation of the full complex Dirac spectrum and spatial transfer growth rates.

</details>


### [67] [Recovering the spatiotemporally dependent diffusion and advection coefficients in a pathway-based Keller--Segel model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23364)
*Yu'an Li,Lingyun Qiu,Min Tang,Hui Yu,Shenwen Yu,Siqin Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies an inverse coefficient problem for an augmented Keller-Segel model of phototactic/chemotactic dynamics, proving Lipschitz stability, cross-sensitivity, and two-stage reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In phototactic and chemotactic population dynamics, the diffusion coefficient D(x,t) and advection coefficient K(x,t) stay time-dependent even in static environments due to intracellular adaptation, relaxing at a common rate. Recovering these coefficients from internal density measurements is important but challenging due to their coupling and compensation effects.

Method: The model is an advection-diffusion equation with space-time dependent diffusion and advection coefficients relaxing exponentially at a shared rate. The authors use internal density measurements to establish conditional Lipschitz stability for recovering one coefficient given the other, derive cross-sensitivity estimates, prove exponentially accurate frozen-coefficient approximation at late times, and prove unique identification of the relaxation rate. They linearize the forward map and propose a two-stage gradient-based reconstruction strategy.

Result: Conditional Lipschitz stability is established for coefficient recovery; cross-sensitivity estimates quantify diffusion-advection compensation. A finite-time separation between transient and steady regimes yields an exponentially accurate frozen-coefficient approximation. Unique identification of the relaxation rate is proven from long-time behavior. Numerical experiments confirm feasibility of the two-stage reconstruction.

Conclusion: The augmented Keller-Segel inverse problem admits stable coefficient recovery with quantified ambiguity, exploits temporal separation between transient and steady regimes, and the proposed two-stage gradient-based method is numerically feasible.

Abstract: We investigate an inverse coefficient problem for an augmented Keller--Segel model arising in the description of phototactic and chemotactic population dynamics. The spatiotemporal evolution of the population density is governed by an advection-diffusion equation in which both the diffusion coefficient and the advection coefficient depend on space and time. Due to the underlying intracellular adaptation mechanism, even in a time-independent external environment, the diffusion coefficient \(D(x,t)\) and the scalar advection coefficient \(K(x,t)\) remain time-dependent and relax exponentially toward their respective steady-state profiles. Importantly, their relaxation is governed by the same rate, determined by the intracellular adaptation dynamics. From internal measurements of the density, we establish conditional Lipschitz stability for recovering either coefficient when the other is known, and derive cross-sensitivity estimates quantifying the compensation between diffusion and advection perturbations that produce the same density data. We further identify a finite-time separation between the transient and steady regimes: the full dynamics admit an exponentially accurate frozen-coefficient approximation at late times. When the steady-state coefficients are known, we also prove unique identification of the relaxation rate from the long-time behavior of the solution. Finally, we derive an explicit linearization of the forward map and, motivated by the temporal separation above, develop a two-stage gradient-based reconstruction strategy. Numerical experiments demonstrate the feasibility of the proposed method.

</details>


### [68] [Global existence of classical solutions to a system modeling propagation of urban crime with quadratic logistic damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23373)
*Minh Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global existence and uniform boundedness of classical solutions to a urban-crime PDE system under no-flux boundary conditions, provided the damping coefficient μ exceeds a specific explicit threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Urban crime models involve chemotaxis-like taxis terms that can drive solutions to blow up. The authors aim to identify conditions on the damping parameter μ that guarantee well-posed global dynamics rather than finite-time singularity.

Method: Analysis of a coupled reaction-diffusion-taxis system with no-flux boundary conditions in a bounded domain Ω⊂R^n (n≥2). The proof relies on deriving a priori estimates and applying PDE regularity theory to bound the solution and prevent blow-up when a large explicit lower bound on μ holds.

Result: If the nonnegative sources B1, B2 are sufficiently regular and μ exceeds the stated explicit threshold (involving χ, n), then for suitably regular initial data the system admits a global classical solution. If additionally inf_{t>0}∫Ω B2 > 0, the solutions are uniformly bounded in time.

Conclusion: Sufficiently strong quadratic damping (large μ) overwhelms the chemotactic aggregation and reaction terms, ensuring global well-posedness and time-uniform boundedness of solutions under a positive-source condition on B2.

Abstract: We are concerned with the following partial differential equations arising from urban crime modeling: \begin{equation} \label{main}
  \begin{cases}
  u_t = Δu - χ\nabla \cdot \left( u \dfrac{\nabla v}{v} \right) - uv + B_1 + ru - μu^2, \\[4pt]
  v_t = Δv - v + uv + B_2,
  \end{cases} \end{equation} under no-flux boundary conditions in a smoothly bounded domain $Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^n$ with $n \geq 2$, where $χ$, $r$, and $μ$ are positive constants. It is shown in this paper that if the nonnegative source terms $B_1$ and $B_2$ are sufficiently regular and \begin{equation*}
  μ> \frac{3}{n} + \frac{1}{n}\left( χn
  + \frac{n(χ(n-1)-2)^2}{4(n-1)} \right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}
  \cdot \left( \frac{(2n+\sqrt{n})^2}{2n-1} \right)^{\frac{1}{n}}
  + n^{12n+3}, \end{equation*} then the system \eqref{main} with suitably regular initial data possesses a global classical solution. Moreover, under the assumption that \begin{equation*} \label{cond.B2}
  \inf_{t>0} \int_ΩB_2(\cdot,t) > 0, \end{equation*} the solutions are uniformly bounded in time.

</details>


### [69] [Riesz transform on manifolds with mixed ends for $1<q<2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23398)
*Bo Li,Tianjun Shen,Zihan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the Riesz transform is bounded on L^q (1<q<2) on a glued manifold formed by connecting several complete manifolds satisfying two-sided Gaussian bounds and volume growth conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend Riesz transform boundedness results to glued manifolds, combining parabolic manifolds under relative connectedness of annuli (RCA) and specific volume growth conditions.

Method: Glue complete manifolds M_1,...,M_l of equal dimension, each satisfying two-sided Gaussian bounds; assume some are parabolic, with polynomial volume growth exponent 1<=n_i<=2, and all satisfy the RCA condition. Then analyze the Riesz transform on the glued manifold.

Result: The Riesz transform ∇L^{-1/2} is bounded on L^q(M) for every 1<q<2 on the gluing manifold M = M_1 # ... # M_l.

Conclusion: Riesz transform L^q boundedness (1<q<2) holds on glued manifolds under Gaussian bounds, suitable volume growth, parabolicity, and RCA conditions.

Abstract: Let $M_1$, $\cdots$, $M_\ell$ be complete manifolds of the same dimension, where $2\le \ell\in\mathbb{N}$. Suppose that each $M_i$ satisfies two side Gaussian bounds. If some of these manifolds are parabolic and there exists a constant $1\le n_i\le 2$ such that for some $x_i\in M_i$ with $1 \le r \le R < \infty$, $$c_i\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{n_i}\le \frac{Vol_{M_i}(B(x_i,R))}{Vol_{M_i}(B(x_i,r))}\le C_i\left(\frac{R}{r}\right)^{n_i},$$ by assuming that all the manifolds satisfy the relative connectedness of the annuli ($RCA$) condition introduced by Grigor'yan and Saloff-Coste, we show that the Riesz transform $\nabla \mathcal L^{-1/2}$ is bounded on $L^q(M)$ for each $1<q<2$ on the gluing manifold $M=M_1 \# M_2 \# \cdots \# M_\ell$.

</details>


### [70] [Continuous sonic-supersonic flows in two-dimensional infinitely long divergent nozzles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23415)
*Lili Qian,Chunpeng Wang,Zihao Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global existence and uniqueness of a continuous sonic-supersonic potential flow in a 2D infinitely long divergent nozzle with straight solid walls, for inlets that are small perturbations of an arc centered at the nozzle vertex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuous sonic-supersonic flows in divergent nozzles are physically important but mathematically challenging due to the degeneracy of the governing equation at the sonic inlet, requiring new techniques to handle it.

Method: Study a quasilinear nonstrictly hyperbolic equation with weak degeneracy at the sonic inlet. Use the method of characteristics to derive precise asymptotic behavior near the sonic inlet for local existence, then extend the local solution globally and derive uniform estimates in the supersonic region.

Result: Establishes global existence and uniqueness of a continuous sonic-supersonic potential flow close to the radially symmetric flow; flow is sonic at inlet (velocity along normal) and becomes supersonic in the nozzle.

Conclusion: The degenerate sonic-supersonic problem in an infinitely long divergent nozzle admits a unique global continuous solution; key tools are asymptotic analysis via characteristics near the sonic inlet and uniform supersonic-region estimates.

Abstract: This paper concerns continuous sonic-supersonic flows in a two-dimensional infinitely long divergent nozzle with straight solid walls. For a given inlet that is a small perturbation of an arc centered at the vertex of the nozzle, we establish the global existence and uniqueness of a continuous sonic-supersonic potential flow which is close to the radially symmetric sonic-supersonic flow. The flow is sonic at the inlet, with its velocity along the normal direction, and becomes supersonic in the nozzle. Such a sonic-supersonic model is governed by a quasilinear nonstrictly hyperbolic equation with degeneracy at the inlet, and the degeneracy is weak in the sense that there exist exactly two distinct characteristics from each sonic point, except for those at the walls, pointing into the supersonic region. One of the crucial ingredients of the analysis is to obtain the precise asymptotic behavior near the sonic inlet by the method of characteristics, which is essential for establishing the local existence of continuous sonic-supersonic flows. Another one is to extend the local solution globally and derive uniform estimates in the supersonic region.

</details>


### [71] [Existence of solutions for a large defocusing parameter in a strongly indefinite Schrödinger equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23446)
*Bartosz Bieganowski*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of nontrivial H^1 solutions to a competing-power (focusing-defocusing) nonlinear Schrödinger equation with periodic potential, where 0 lies in a finite spectral gap of L=-Δ+V. For large λ, solutions exist with norm bounded by Cλ^{-1/(q-2)}, and rescaled/translated solutions converge weakly to a solution of the pure defocusing equation Lv=-|v|^{q-2}v.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Study nonlinear Schrödinger equations with competing focusing (|u|^{p-2}u) and defocusing (-λ|u|^{q-2}u) power nonlinearities in the presence of a periodic potential. The key structural assumption is that 0 belongs to a finite spectral gap of the periodic Schrödinger operator, a setting that yields distinctive spectral and variational features motivating the search for solutions localized near the gap.

Method: Variational/analysis of the competing-power equation -Δu+V(x)u=|u|^{p-2}u-λ|u|^{q-2}u in R^N, with 2<q<p<2^*, V real Z^N-periodic and bounded, and 0 in a finite spectral gap of L=-Δ+V. Small-norm solutions obtained for large λ via rescaling and lattice translations, plus a limiting/variational argument linking to the pure defocusing equation Lv=-|v|^{q-2}v.

Result: There exists λ_∞>0 such that for all λ≥λ_∞, the equation has a nontrivial solution u_λ∈H^1(R^N), with the bound ‖u_λ‖_{H^1}+‖u_λ‖_{L^∞} ≤ Cλ^{-1/(q-2)}. Moreover, after natural rescaling and lattice translations, a sequence of such solutions converges weakly to a nontrivial solution of the pure defocusing equation Lv=-|v|^{q-2}v.

Conclusion: For large λ, the defocusing term dominates, producing small solutions whose amplitude decays like λ^{-1/(q-2)}, and the asymptotic profile is governed by the defocusing limit equation Lv=-|v|^{q-2}v. The finite spectral gap assumption on L is essential in constructing these solutions.

Abstract: Let $N\geq 3$, $2<q<p<2^*$, and let $V\in L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^N)$ be real-valued and $\mathbb{Z}^N$-periodic. We assume that $0$ belongs to a finite spectral gap of the periodic Schrödinger operator $L=-Δ+V$. We prove that there exists $λ_\infty>0$ such that, for every $λ\geqλ_\infty$, the competing-power equation $$ -Δu+V(x)u=|u|^{p-2}u-λ|u|^{q-2}u \quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^N $$ has a nontrivial solution $u_λ\in H^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$. Moreover, $$ \|u_λ\|_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^N)}+\|u_λ\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^N)} \leq Cλ^{-1/(q-2)}. $$ We also show that, after the natural rescaling and lattice translations, a sequence of such solutions converges weakly to a nontrivial solution of the pure defocusing equation $Lv=-|v|^{q-2}v$.

</details>


### [72] [Nonlinear stability of composite waves of traveling wave and rarefaction wave for a parabolic-hyperbolic system arising from chemotaxis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23448)
*Sitong Liu,Jingyu Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves nonlinear stability of a composite wave (traveling wave + rarefaction wave) for a parabolic-hyperbolic chemotaxis system under small H^1 perturbation, showing global convergence with an absolutely continuous shift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Composite waves combining viscous shocks and rarefaction waves arise in parabolic-hyperbolic chemotaxis systems, but their nonlinear stability is not well understood, especially when the two waves interact via non-exact superposition.

Method: Combines a weighted relative-entropy mechanism for viscous shocks with the energy structure of rarefaction waves. Introduces a rarefaction modulation factor in the weighted relative entropy whose derivatives, together with rarefaction-profile terms, generate rarefaction dissipation. Handles interaction errors via spatial wave separation and closes the full H^1 estimate by exploiting the system coupling to recover missing dissipation in the hyperbolic component.

Result: A global-in-time solution exists and converges toward the composite wave with an absolutely continuous shift, provided the initial data is a small H^1-type perturbation.

Conclusion: Nonlinear stability of the composite wave holds; the rarefaction modulation factor and exploitation of the coupling structure are key to closing the energy estimates despite diffusion acting only on density.

Abstract: We study the nonlinear stability of a composite wave consisting of a traveling wave and a rarefaction wave for a parabolic-hyperbolic system arising from chemotaxis. We prove that if the initial value is a small $H^1$-type perturbation of composite wave, then the system admits a global solution that converges toward the composite wave with an absolutely continuous shift. The proof combines the weighted relative-entropy mechanism for viscous shocks with the energy structure of rarefaction waves. A key ingredient is the inclusion of a rarefaction modulation factor in the weighted relative entropy; its derivatives combine with the terms generated by the rarefaction profile to produce a rarefaction dissipation. Moreover, the spatial separation of the two waves yields time-integrable interaction errors caused by the non-exact superposition. Since diffusion acts only on the density component, the full $H^1$ estimate is closed by exploiting the coupling structure of the system to recover the missing dissipation of the hyperbolic component.

</details>


### [73] [Directional Tangency and Anomalous Far-Field Decay for Schr{ö}dinger Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23472)
*Ruming Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper identifies a "directional tangency mechanism" that explains anomalous far-field decay in Schrödinger equations, driven by geometric alignment between the Fermi surface, source singularity, and observation direction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard far-field decay in Schrödinger equations can exhibit anomalous behavior not captured by conventional asymptotics. The paper aims to pinpoint the geometric mechanism responsible for such anomalies.

Method: For constant-coefficient operators, the authors introduce a directional decomposition of the limiting resolvent using the geometry of the Fermi surface and observation direction. They analyze how analytic vs. non-analytic source singularities interact with the directional tangency set, and then construct a bounded decaying potential to realize the same mechanism in the first Born correction via the effective source Vu0.

Result: In 2D, an analytic source yields super-algebraically small tangency contribution, while a transverse non-analyticity on the tangency set gives an algebraic contribution of order R^{-(2α+1)}. Shifting the singularity off the tangency set restores super-algebraic decay. For the constructed potential, in a long-range regime the tangency contribution decays more slowly than the conventional 2D far-field term.

Conclusion: Geometric alignment (tangency) between source singularity and observation direction is essential to anomalous far-field decay, and this mechanism persists in scattering with a suitable potential.

Abstract: We identify a directional tangency mechanism responsible for anomalous far-field decay in Schrödinger equations. For constant-coefficient operators, we introduce a directional decomposition of the limiting resolvent based on the geometry of the Fermi surface and the observation direction. In two dimensions, we show that an analytic source produces a super-algebraically small tangency contribution, whereas a transverse non-analyticity located on the directional tangency set generates an algebraic contribution of order \(R^{-(2α+1)}\). Shifting the singularity away from the tangency set restores super-algebraic decay, showing that geometric alignment is essential to the anomalous behavior. We further construct a bounded, decaying real-valued potential for which the same mechanism occurs in the first Born correction through the effective source \(Vu_0\). In a suitable long-range regime, the resulting tangency contribution decays more slowly than the conventional two-dimensional far-field term.

</details>


### [74] [Mixed Grushin Heat Equations with Riesz-Potential Nonlinearities in Marcinkiewicz Spaces: Subcritical, Critical, and Supercritical Regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23481)
*Aparajita Dasgupta,Uttam Kumar Dolai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a well-posedness theory in Marcinkiewicz spaces for a nonlinear evolution equation driven by a mixed Grushin operator plus a nonlocal potential source, establishing smoothing estimates and existence/blow-up results across subcritical, critical, and supercritical regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior results largely rely on Lebesgue-space frameworks, excluding initial data with singular profiles. This work aims to handle singular data and to understand how two competing diffusion scales (second-order Grushin and fractional perturbation) interact to determine admissible integrability regimes.

Method: Uses spectral calculus, subordination, and interpolation to derive Lebesgue and Lorentz smoothing estimates for the mixed semigroup e^{-t(G+G^δ)}, distinguishing short-time second-order behaviour from large-time fractional decay. Combines a Yamazaki-type estimate with Lorentz duality and the potential-operator kernel structure.

Result: Subcritical regime (ρ<1+p/β): local existence, uniqueness, Lipschitz dependence, and blow-up alternative in L^{p,∞}. Critical (ρ=1+p/β): global mild solutions for small L^{p,∞} data. Supercritical (ρ>1+p/β): well-posedness and small-data global existence in higher-integrability spaces L^{q,∞} with β(ρ−1)<q<β_δ(ρ−1). Solutions attain initial data in weak-* sense.

Conclusion: The results extend Marcinkiewicz-space theory to mixed Grushin diffusion with spatially nonlocal potential source, revealing the role of the two competing diffusion scales in setting admissible integrability regimes.

Abstract: We study the nonlinear evolution equation $$ \partial_t u+(G+G^δ)u=I_α(|u|^ρ) \quad\text{on }\mathbb{R}^{N+k}, $$ where $G$ is the nonnegative self adjoint realization of Grushin operator, $0<δ<1$, and $I_α$ is the potential operator with kernel $|z|^{-α}$, $0<α<N+k$. We develop a well-posedness theory for initial data in Marcinkiewicz spaces, allowing singular profiles outside the corresponding Lebesgue spaces. Using spectral calculus, subordination, and interpolation, we establish Lebesgue and Lorentz smoothing estimates for the mixed semigroup $e^{-t(G+G^δ)}$, which distinguish the second-order behaviour at short times from the fractional decay at large times. In the subcritical regime, considering $$ \frac1β=\frac2Q+1-\fracα{d}, \qquad \frac1{β_δ}=\frac{2δ}{Q}+1-\fracα{d}, $$ where $Q$ is homogenous dimension, we prove local existence, uniqueness, Lipschitz dependence on the initial data, and a blow-up alternative in $L^{p,\infty}$ when $$ ρ<1+\frac{p}β. $$ At the critical relation $ρ=1+p/β$, a Yamazaki-type estimate and Lorentz duality yield global mild solutions for sufficiently small data in $L^{p,\infty}$. In the supercritical regime $ρ>1+p/β$, well-posedness is recovered in higher-integrability Marcinkiewicz spaces $L^{q,\infty}$ satisfying $$ β(ρ-1)<q<β_δ(ρ-1), $$ with global existence for sufficiently small initial data. In all cases, the solutions attain their initial data in the weak-$*$ sense. These results extend the Marcinkiewicz-space theory to mixed Grushin diffusion with a spatially nonlocal potential source and reveal the role of the two competing diffusion scales in determining the admissible integrability regimes.

</details>


### [75] [A brief introduction to the regularity theory of minimising harmonic maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23497)
*Siran Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A survey-style paper presenting classical foundational results on the regularity theory of harmonic maps, focusing on energy-minimising and stationary harmonic maps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consolidate and present classical foundational results on harmonic map regularity theory in a coherent exposition, centered on energy-minimising harmonic maps.

Method: Expository treatment of key tools and results: inner and outer variations, the monotonicity identity, the epsilon-regularity theorem, blow-ups and tangent maps, and the dimension estimate and stratification of singular sets; also parallels for stationary harmonic maps.

Result: A structured account of the classical regularity theory, including the main theorems and techniques that underlie the analysis of singular sets and their structure.

Conclusion: Serves as an introduction/reference to harmonic map regularity, extending to stationary harmonic maps and noting significant recent developments.

Abstract: We introduce various classical, foundational results on the regularity theory of harmonic maps, with focuses on the energy minimising harmonic maps. Topics include inner and outer variations, the monotonicity identity, the $\varepsilon$-regularity theorem, blow-ups and tangent maps, and the dimension estimate and stratification of singular sets. We also discuss the analogous theory for stationary harmonic maps and mention some significant recent developments.

</details>


### [76] [Finite-data inverse nodal optimization in angular-momentum sectors of Schrödinger operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23502)
*Xijun Deng,Zhisu Liu,Yonghui Xia*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a finite-data inverse nodal optimization problem for radial Schrödinger operators on a ball in R^d, using a Friedrichs-endpoint formulation in weighted L^p space. It establishes differentiability of nodal radii, proves existence of minimum-distance potentials, derives critical equations, and obtains submersion/uniqueness results, including global optimizer existence for p>(d+2)/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inverse nodal problems for radial Schrödinger operators are typically analyzed via a regular one-dimensional reduction. The authors retain the singular radial geometry directly (Friedrichs endpoint, weighted L_d^p) to treat the problem in its physical setting.

Method: Uses a Volterra representation of the Friedrichs branch; establishes weak continuity and continuous Fréchet differentiability of fixed nodal radii with respect to the potential; proves existence of minimum-distance potentials and derives critical Schrödinger equations; analyzes linear independence of nodal gradients, submersion structure, observation principle, and transversality.

Result: Obtains weak continuity and Fréchet differentiability of nodal radii; existence of minimum-distance potentials and critical equations; linear independence of nodal gradients for several nodes giving sharp local minimum-norm reconstruction; explicit inverse-Gram formulas and local uniqueness in the Hilbert case; global optimizer existence for inward node displacements when ℓ=0, constant potential, second radial mode, and p>(d+2)/2.

Conclusion: The Friedrichs-endpoint weighted-space framework enables a rigorous inverse nodal optimization theory for radial Schrödinger operators, yielding differentiability, reconstruction, and uniqueness results plus a global optimizer existence theorem in a Sobolev-type regime.

Abstract: In this paper, we study a finite-data inverse nodal optimization problem for radial Schrödinger operators $$
  H_q:=-Δ+q(|x|),\qquad u|_{\partial B_R}=0,
  \qquad d\geq2, $$ on the ball $B_R\subset\mathbb R^d$, in an arbitrary fixed angular-momentum sector. The analysis is built directly at the Friedrichs endpoint and in the physical weighted space $L_d^p$, $p>d/2$, so that the singular radial geometry is retained rather than replaced by a regular one-dimensional model. By means of a Volterra representation of the Friedrichs branch, we establish weak continuity and continuous Fréchet differentiability of fixed nodal radii with respect to the potential. We then prove existence of minimum-distance potentials and derive the corresponding critical Schrödinger equations. For several nodes of the same eigenfunction, the nodal gradients are linearly independent, which yields a finite-dimensional submersion structure and sharp local minimum-norm reconstruction. A general observation principle is further used to treat mixed angular-momentum data under the corresponding transversality condition and simultaneous spectral--nodal data from one eigenmode, for which transversality is automatic; in the Hilbert case this gives explicit inverse-Gram formulas and local uniqueness. Finally, when $\ell=0$, the reference potential is constant, and the second radial mode is considered, we prove that every inward displacement of the unique interior node admits a unique global optimizer for $p>(d+2)/2$.

</details>


### [77] [Sharp criteria for the existence of solutions to curvature equations with two singular sources on rectangular torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23531)
*Zhijie Chen,Zhen Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a curvature (mean-field) equation with two singular sources on a flat torus, focusing on the general case $\wp'(2p)\neq 0$ left open by Kuo (2026), and establishes a sharp existence/non-existence criterion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kuo (J. Differential Geom. 2026) solved only the special case $\wp'(2p)=0$; the general case $\wp'(2p)\neq 0$ remained open. In this general case the Hill discriminant of the associated generalized Lamé equation acquires an essential singularity, complicating the analysis.

Method: The authors analyze the curvature equation $\Delta u+e^u=8\pi(\delta_p+\delta_{-p})$ on $E_\tau=\mathbb{C}/(\mathbb{Z}+\mathbb{Z}\tau)$. They develop new ideas to determine the intersection of two conditional stability sets completely, despite the essential singularity of the Hill discriminant.

Result: Complete determination of the intersection of the two conditional stability sets, yielding a sharp criterion for existence and non-existence of solutions when $E_\tau$ is a rectangular torus and $\wp(p)\in\mathbb{R}$.

Conclusion: The intersection of the stability sets is exactly characterized despite the essential singularity, providing a sharp existence/non-existence criterion in the rectangular-torus real case.

Abstract: We study the following curvature equation with two singular sources on a flat torus
  $$
  Δu+e^u=8π(δ_p+δ_{-p})\quad\text{on } E_τ:=\mathbb{C}/(\mathbb{Z}+\mathbb{Z}τ),
  $$
  where $δ_p$ denotes the Dirac measure at $p$. The case $\wp'(2p)=0$ was solved by Kuo (J. Differential Geom. 2026). In this paper, we study the general case $\wp'(2p)\neq 0$. A novel phenomenon for $\wp'(2p)\neq 0$ is that the Hill discriminant of the associated generalized Lamé equation has an essential singularity. We develop new ideas to determine the intersection of two conditional stability sets completely, which is quite surprising since it seems impossible to determine the exact structure of these two sets due to the essential singularity. This leads to a sharp criteria for the existence and non-existence of solutions when $E_τ$ is a rectangular torus and $\wp(p)\in \mathbb{R}$.

</details>


### [78] [The solvability of the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gauss Minkowski problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23588)
*Na Fu,Jian-Ping Sun,Bin Chen,Ya-Hong Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gauss Minkowski problem via $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gaussian curvature measures, solving an associated Monge-Ampère type equation on the sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This paper investigates the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gauss Minkowski problem associated with the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gaussian curvature measure.

Method: The problem's solvability is reduced to a class of Monge-Ampère type equations on the unit sphere. Under smooth assumptions, the authors analyze existence and uniqueness conditions.

Result: Existence of solutions is established for $p<1$, $q<0$, and $q<p$; uniqueness is obtained for $p\geq q$. A complete solution to the existence part is achieved for $p<1$ and $q<0$.

Conclusion: The $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gauss Minkowski problem is fully resolved in its existence aspect for $p<1$, $q<0$, with uniqueness for $p\geq q$.

Abstract: This paper investigates the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gauss Minkowski problem associated with the $L_p$-dual mixed Euclidean-Gaussian curvature measure. The solvability of this problem is equivalent to that of a class of Monge-Ampère type equations on the unit sphere. Under smooth assumptions, We establish the existence of solutions for $p<1$, $q<0$, and $q<p$ and uniqueness of solutions for $p\geq q$ to the aforementioned Monge-Ampère type equations, respectively. Furthermore, we obtain a complete solution concerning the existence part of this problem when $p<1$ and $q<0$.

</details>


### [79] [Dirichlet Eigenvalues for the Logarithmic-Hardy operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23593)
*Tangrui Liao,Ying Wang,Minghua Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a variational spectral theory for the Dirichlet eigenvalue problem of the logarithmic Hardy operator in a bounded Lipschitz domain containing the origin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spectral theory for the logarithmic Laplacian with a critical logarithmic potential (logarithmic Hardy operator) is largely unexplored, especially regarding compactness and well-posedness depending on the perturbation parameter.

Method: Establish a logarithmic Hardy inequality to identify the energy space; analyze compactness of the embedding into L² as a function of the perturbation parameter; for parameters above −1, develop a variational spectral theory via successive minimizations over orthogonal complements.

Result: The L² embedding is compact when the perturbation parameter exceeds −1, but fails at the endpoint −1. For parameters above −1, the problem admits a discrete sequence of eigenvalues tending to infinity, with eigenfunctions forming a complete orthonormal basis of L².

Conclusion: A uniform lower bound for the first eigenvalue and a scaling property under domain dilation are established, determining a critical radius for the sign of the first eigenvalue while leaving eigenvalue gaps invariant.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the Dirichlet eigenvalue problem for the logarithmic Hardy operator, defined as the logarithmic Laplacian with a critical logarithmic potential, in a bounded Lipschitz domain containing the origin. We first establish a logarithmic Hardy inequality and use it to identify the associated energy space, showing that the embedding into \(L^2\) is compact when the perturbation parameter exceeds the endpoint value \(-1\), but fails to be compact at that endpoint. For parameters above \(-1\), we develop a complete variational spectral theory, prove that the problem admits a discrete sequence of eigenvalues tending to infinity, characterized by successive minimizations over orthogonal complements, whose eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis of \(L^2\).
  We further establish a uniform lower bound for the first eigenvalue, a scaling property under domain dilation that determines a critical radius for the sign of the first eigenvalue and leaves the eigenvalue gaps invariant.

</details>


### [80] [Enhanced lifespan of solutions to Whitham-Boussinesq system with surface tension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23612)
*Madina Kamet,Achenef Tesfahun*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Well-posedness of a Whitham-Boussinesq system with surface tension (β>1/3) on long time scales depending explicitly on nonlinearity, shallowness, and surface tension parameters, plus results for the classical Whitham equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Surface tension effects matter in shallow-water bi-directional wave propagation, yet explicit lifespan dependence on all governing parameters (nonlinearity ε, shallowness μ, surface tension β) for such Whitham-Boussinesq models is not fully understood.

Method: Dispersive and Strichartz estimates combined with energy estimates, under a non-cavitation condition with parameter h₀.

Result: Well-posedness for β>1/3 up to times of order (β*^{1/4} μ^{1/4} h₀ ε^{-1})^{4/3} in 1D and (β*^{1/4} μ^{1/4} h₀ ε^{-1})^{2-} in 2D, with β*=min(|3β-1|,1/2). Also well-posedness for the classical Whitham equation (β=0 or β>1/3) on timescale (β*^{1/4} μ^{1/4} ε^{-1})^{4/3}.

Conclusion: The results explicitly quantify how existence time depends on all parameters, giving long-time well-posedness for shallow-water Whitham-Boussinesq models with surface tension.

Abstract: We consider a Whitham-Boussinesq type system with surface tension arising as asymptotic models in the bi-directional propagation of weakly nonlinear surface waves in shallow water, which is characterized by a nonlinearity parameter $0<ε\le 1$, a shallow water parameter $0<μ\le 1$ and a nonnegative surface tension parameter $β$. In this paper, we establish well-posedness of the associated Cauchy problem for $β>1/3$ on a time scale of order $ ( β_\ast^{ 1/4} μ^{ 1/4} h_0 ε^{-1} )^{4/3}$ in one dimension and of order $( β_\ast^{ 1/4} μ^{ 1/4} h_0 ε^{-1})^{2-}$ in two dimensions, where $β_\ast= \min(|3β-1|, 1/2)$, assuming a non-cavitation condition with a parameter $h_0>0$. In addition, we established well-posedness for the classical Whitham equation with either $β=0$ or $β>1/3$ on the timescale of order $ ( β_\ast^{ 1/4} μ^{ 1/4} ε^{-1} )^{4/3}$. These results explicitly capture how the existence time depends on all the parameters involved. Our proofs rely on dispersive and Strichartz estimates combined with energy estimates.

</details>


### [81] [Complete integrability of the Calogero--Sutherland DNLS equation on $L^2_+(\mathbb T)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23625)
*Rana Badreddine*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves complete integrability of the defocusing Calogero-Sutherland derivative nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the torus via a nonlinear Fourier transform, yielding explicit linear flow and almost periodic dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The defocusing Calogero-Sutherland derivative NLS equation is a physically significant integrable system, but its complete integrability and long-time dynamical behavior on the torus require rigorous justification. The authors aim to establish a nonlinear Fourier transform that linearizes the flow and characterizes its dynamics.

Method: They construct a nonlinear Fourier transform Φ mapping L^2_+(T) to ℓ^2(N_0), built from the eigenvalues, spectral gaps γ_n = λ_n - λ_{n-1} - 1 ≥ 0, and eigenfunctions of the Lax operator L_u. They prove Φ is a norm-preserving homeomorphism and show the flow becomes an explicit rotation ζ_n(t) = e^{-iω_n t} ζ_n(0), with frequencies ω_n depending only on the conserved spectrum.

Result: Every orbit is precompact in L^2_+(T), and t ↦ u(t) is Bohr almost periodic. For finite-gap data, the authors obtain quasi-periodicity in time and a sharp criterion for genuine periodicity.

Conclusion: The defocusing Calogero-Sutherland derivative NLS is completely integrable, with dynamics fully linearized by the nonlinear Fourier transform. This yields precompactness, almost periodicity, and sharp periodicity criteria for finite-gap solutions.

Abstract: We establish the complete integrability of the defocusing Calogero--Sutherland derivative nonlinear Schrödinger equation \begin{equation}\label{CS-abstract}\tag{CS}
  i\partial_t u +\partial_x^2 u + 2i u \partial_x\,Π\!\left(|u|^2\right)=0,\qquad x\in\mathbb T, \end{equation} by constructing a nonlinear Fourier transform \begin{equation*}
  Φ: u\in L^2_+(\mathbb T)\mapsto\ (ζ_n(u))_{n\geq 0}\in \ell^2(\mathbb N_0),\qquad
  ζ_n(u)=\sqrt{γ_n(u)}\;e^{\,i\arg\langle u\mid f_n\rangle}, \end{equation*} built from the eigenvalues $(λ_n)$, the spectral gaps $γ_n:=λ_n-λ_{n-1}-1\geq 0$ and the eigenfunctions $(f_n)$ of the Lax operator $L_u$. We prove that $Φ$ is a norm-preserving homeomorphism. In these coordinates, the flow becomes the explicit rotation $ζ_n(t)=e^{-iω_n t}ζ_n(0)$ for all $n$, whose frequencies $ω_n$ depend only on the conserved spectrum $(λ_n)$. As consequences, every orbit of \eqref{CS-abstract} is precompact in $L^2_+(\mathbb T)$, and $t\mapsto u(t)$ is Bohr almost periodic. For finite-gap data, we obtain quasi-periodicity in time and a sharp criterion for genuine periodicity.

</details>


### [82] [On inverse scattering for the one-dimensional nonlinear Dirac equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23628)
*Hironobu Sasaki*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper solves the inverse scattering problem for the 1D nonlinear Dirac equation, reconstructing higher-order derivatives of the nonlinearities at zero from the scattering operator, via a new massless-limit method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inverse scattering results exist for nonlinear Schrödinger (Sasaki 2024) and nonlinear Klein-Gordon (Sasaki 2025) equations, but their methods fail for the nonlinear Dirac equation due to its system nature—specifically the hard problem of proving invertibility of a matrix tied to input data.

Method: Assumes smooth nonlinearities with prescribed vanishing order near zero. Introduces a new approach based on the massless limit for the free solutions to circumvent the matrix-invertibility difficulty, then derives a reconstruction formula for derivatives of the nonlinearities at the origin (order ≥5) from the scattering operator.

Result: Under additional assumptions on the nonlinearities, a reconstruction formula is established for (∂ᵅ N_j)(0) with |α|≥5 using knowledge of the scattering operator.

Conclusion: The inverse scattering problem for the 1D nonlinear Dirac equation is solvable, extending prior Sasaki results to systems through a novel massless-limit technique.

Abstract: The inverse scattering problem for the one-dimensional nonlinear Dirac equation \begin{align*} \begin{cases} i(u_t+u_x)+v=\mathcal{N}_1(u,v),\\ i(v_t-v_x)+u=\mathcal{N}_2(u,v) \end{cases} \end{align*} is studied. We assume that the unknown nonlinearities $\mathcal{N}_j(u,v)$ ($j=1,2$) of the equation belong to $C^\infty(\mathbb{C}^2;\mathbb{C})$ and satisfy $(\partial_\mathbf{z}^α\mathcal{N}_j)(\mathbf{z})=O(|\mathbf{z}|^{\max\{ 5-|α|,0 \}})$ ($|\mathbf{z}|\to 0$) for any multi-index $α\in (\mathbb{N} \cup \{ 0 \})^4$. Here, $\mathbf{z}=(z_1,z_2)\in \mathbb{C}^2$, $
\partial_\mathbf{z}^α= (\partial_{z_1},\partial_{\overline{z_1}},\partial_{z_2},\partial_{\overline{z_2}})^α, $
and $\partial_{z_1},\partial_{\overline{z_1}},\partial_{z_2},\partial_{\overline{z_2}}$ are the Wirtinger differential operators. Under some additional assumptions on $\mathcal{N}_j$, we establish a reconstruction formula for $(\partial_\mathbf{z}^α\mathcal{N}_j)(0)$ ($|α|\ge 5$) using the knowledge of the scattering operator for the equation. Although such results were established for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation [Sasaki 2024] and the nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation [Sasaki 2025], the methods in [Sasaki 2024, 2025] are not directly applicable to the nonlinear Dirac equation due to its system nature. Specifically, it is quite difficult to prove the invertibility of a matrix associated with the input data. To overcome this difficulty, we introduce a new method based on the massless limit for the free solutions.

</details>


### [83] [Blow-up for energy supercritical defocusing nonlinear Schrödinger equations in dimensions three, four and five](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23685)
*Kevin Buck,Alvaro Carballeira,Javier Gómez-Serrano,Jia Shi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Finite-time blow-up is proved for the energy supercritical defocusing nonlinear Schrödinger equation in dimensions 3, 4, 5, with asymptotically self-similar solutions and diverging critical Sobolev norm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This closes Bourgain's conjecture (2000) on global well-posedness and scattering for the defocusing energy supercritical NLS in dimensions three and four.

Method: A computer-assisted shooting argument applied to smooth, radial, compactly supported initial data.

Result: Blow-up occurs for (d,p) = (3,27), (4,9), (5,7); solutions are asymptotically self-similar, with L^infinity norm growing at the scaling rate (T-t)^{-1/(p-1)} and divergent critical Sobolev norm H^{s_c}.

Conclusion: The defocusing energy supercritical NLS exhibits finite-time blow-up, disproving global well-posedness in these cases.

Abstract: We prove finite time blow-up from smooth, radial, compactly supported initial data for the energy supercritical defocusing nonlinear Schrödinger equation $i\partial_tu+Δu-|u|^{p-1}u=0$ in $\mathbb R^d$, for $(d,p)\in\{(3,27),(4,9),(5,7)\}$. The solutions are asymptotically self-similar, their $L^\infty$ norm grows at the scaling rate $(T-t)^{-1/(p-1)}$, and their critical Sobolev norm $\dot H^{s_c}$ diverges. This closes the conjecture of Bourgain \cite{Bourgain2000} on global well-posedness and scattering for the defocusing energy supercritical equation in dimensions three and four. The proof relies on a shooting argument and is computer-assisted.

</details>


### [84] [Quantitative propagation of chaos in total variation for unregularized Vlasov--Riesz--Fokker--Planck systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23709)
*Ning Jiang,Juntao Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves quantitative propagation of chaos in total variation for the unregularized 3D repulsive Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck particle system, showing convergence of the N-particle law to the nonlinear limit at rate N^{-b} for any b<1/3, and extends results to repulsive Riesz potentials with singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior propagation of chaos results often rely on regularized forces or fail to provide quantitative rates for singular/unregularized interactions in total variation. This work aims to handle the unregularized (singular) VPFP system directly.

Method: Combines conditioning of the product initial law, auxiliary k-particle Fokker-Planck equations, and weighted L^q estimates for the BBGKY hierarchy. The level-(k+1) interaction term is controlled via a weighted Hölder estimate under a local integrability condition K in L^{q'}_{loc}. On R^d, polynomial spatial weights control the far field.

Result: For tensorized initial data with weighted Sobolev regularity and a Gaussian moment, for every 0<b<1/3 there exist τ_b>0 and C_b≥1 independent of N and k such that sup_{0≤t≤τ_b} ||F_{N,k}(t) - f_t^{⊗k}||_TV ≤ C_b^k N^{-b}. More generally, for repulsive Riesz potentials |x|^{-s} on T^d and R^d with 0<s<d/2, the rate is C^k(N^{-1/q} + N^{-(d/s-2)}) for q≥2 and q>d/(d-s-1).

Conclusion: The singular particle dynamics are globally well posed without collisions for each fixed particle number, allowing the force regularization to be removed. Quantitative propagation of chaos is established for singular repulsive interactions.

Abstract: We establish quantitative propagation of chaos in total variation for the unregularized three-dimensional repulsive Vlasov--Poisson--Fokker--Planck (VPFP) particle system. For tensorized initial data satisfying weighted Sobolev regularity and a Gaussian moment, we prove that for every $0<b<1/3$ there exist $τ_b>0$ and $C_b\ge1$, independent of $N$ and $k$, such that \[
  \sup_{0\le t\leτ_b}\|F_{N,k}(t)-f_t^{\otimes k}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
  \le C_b^kN^{-b},
  \qquad N\ge1,\quad 1\le k\le N. \] More generally, for repulsive Riesz potentials with singularity $|x|^{-s}$ on $\mathbb{T}^d$ and $\mathbb{R}^d$, $0<s<d/2$, we obtain $C^k\bigl(N^{-1/q}+N^{-(d/s-2)}\bigr)$ for $q\ge2$ and $q>d/(d-s-1)$. The proof combines conditioning of the product initial law, auxiliary $k$-particle Fokker--Planck equations, and weighted $L^q$ estimates for the BBGKY hierarchy. The level-$(k+1)$ interaction term is controlled by a weighted Hölder estimate under the local condition $K\in L^{q'}_{\mathrm{loc}}$; for $d=3$ and $s=1$, this condition is $q>3$. On $\mathbb{R}^d$, polynomial spatial weights control the far field. For each fixed particle number, the singular particle dynamics are globally well posed and have no collisions. This permits the force regularization to be removed.

</details>


### [85] [On the compactness of artificial compressibility approximations of weak solutions for fluid problems in deforming domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23738)
*Stephan Schmitz,Anna Hundertmark*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a time-equicontinuity estimate for weak solutions of the artificial compressibility approximation to Newtonian fluid flow on deforming domains, independent of the compressibility parameter, enabling convergence to the incompressible limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Artificial compressibility is a common approach for computing incompressible flows, but proving convergence as the compressibility vanishes requires a compactness argument (typically the Aubin–Lions lemma). On deforming domains, such an argument is complicated by the domain changing in time, which the authors address.

Method: The problem is remapped onto a fixed reference domain. The estimate is derived using appropriate divergence-preserving test functions that involve the difference of two solutions at different time points, defined with respect to different domains/coordinates.

Result: An estimate on the integral equicontinuity in time of the weak solutions is established. It is independent of the compressibility parameter.

Conclusion: This estimate serves as an alternative compactness argument for the convergence of weak-solution sequences in the vanishing-compressibility (incompressible) limit on general deforming domains, in two and three space dimensions.

Abstract: In this contribution, a fluid flow problem on a general deforming domain for a Newtonian fluid in two and three space dimensions with artificial compressibility approximation is studied. We prove an estimate on the integral equicontinuity in time of the weak solutions under suitable domain regularity assumptions, which is independent on the compressibility parameter and serves as an alternative compactness argument for the convergence of weak solution sequences for vanishing compressibility.
  The corresponding estimate is obtained by remapping the problem onto a fixed reference domain and using appropriate divergence-preserving testfunctions involving the difference of two solutions at different points in time, thus defined with respect to different domains/coordinates.

</details>


### [86] [Explicit diagonalization of the linearized Calderón problem on the disk: multiplicity normalization and exponential spectral attenuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23740)
*William Robert Breckon Lionheart*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper provides an explicit continuous spectral diagonalization of the normal operator for the linearized Schrödinger form of the Calderón problem on the unit disk, yielding a generalized singular system for the linearized forward map.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the linearized Calderón problem's measurement map and its spectral structure is important for inversion and reconstruction. Prior work lacked a full angularly dependent, multiplicity-normalized diagonalization on the disk.

Method: The authors study the full angular dependence of the linearized Schrödinger form of the Calderón problem on the unit disk and explicitly diagonalize the normal operator's spectrum continuously.

Result: An explicit continuous spectral diagonalization of the normal operator is obtained, giving a generalized singular system for the linearized forward map—claimed to be the first such diagonalization for the fully angularly dependent, multiplicity-normalized linearized measurement map on the disk.

Conclusion: The work establishes a novel spectral/generalized singular decomposition for the linearized Calderón problem on the disk, enabling a fuller angular analysis of the measurement map.

Abstract: We study the full angular dependence of the linearized Schrödinger form of the Calderón problem on the unit disk. We obtain an explicit continuous spectral diagonalization of its normal operator and hence a generalized singular system for the linearized forward map. To our knowledge, this is the first such diagonalization for the full angularly dependent, multiplicity-normalized linearized measurement map on the disk.

</details>


### [87] [Global Existence and Large Time Asymptotic Behavior of Strong Solutions to the Two-Dimensional Nematic Liquid Crystal Flows with Large Initial Data and Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23760)
*Qinghao Lei,Lu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global existence and decay of strong solutions for 2D nonhomogeneous incompressible nematic liquid crystal flows with vanishing density, without geometric angle conditions on the initial orientation field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior studies on 2D nematic liquid crystal flows often require the initial orientation field to satisfy geometric angle conditions or impose restrictions on initial data. This work aims to remove such constraints and handle cases where density is allowed to vanish.

Method: The authors study the 2D nonhomogeneous incompressible nematic liquid crystal flow system. They exploit smallness of the $L^q$-norm of $\nabla \mu(\rho_0)$ (for some $q>2$) with density-dependent viscosity to prove global existence and exponential decay of strong solutions in bounded domains, and extend to Cauchy problems with constant viscosity and either vacuum or non-vacuum far-field density.

Result: Global existence and exponential decay of strong solutions are obtained for vanishing initial density with small $\|\nabla \mu(\rho_0)\|_{L^q}$. Global existence with large initial data is achieved for constant viscosity. For Cauchy problems with constant viscosity, global existence and large-time decay rates hold for arbitrarily large initial data, with no geometric angle condition needed on the initial orientation field.

Conclusion: The paper provides a unified framework for global well-posedness and decay of 2D nonhomogeneous incompressible nematic liquid crystal flows, removing geometric angle conditions and allowing vacuum, thus generalizing existing results.

Abstract: This paper investigates the two-dimensional nonhomogeneous incompressible nematic liquid crystal flows. For initial density allowed to vanish, we first establish the global existence and exponential decay of strong solutions in bounded domains with density-dependent viscosity, under the assumption that the $L^q$-norm of $\nabla μ(ρ_0)$ is sufficiently small for some $q>2$. As a consequence, we obtain the global existence of strong solutions with large initial data for constant viscosity. Furthermore, for the Cauchy problem with constant viscosity and either vacuum or non-vacuum far-field density, we prove the global existence and large-time decay rates of strong solutions for arbitrarily large initial data. It is worth noting that our results do not require the initial orientation field to satisfy any geometric angle condition.

</details>


### [88] [Global Existence and Large-Time Behavior of Strong Solutions to the Two-Dimensional Compressible Nematic Liquid Crystal Flows with Large Initial Data and Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23764)
*Qinghao Lei,Lu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence, uniqueness, and exponential decay of strong solutions to 2D compressible nematic liquid crystal flows with density-dependent bulk viscosity λ(ρ)=ρ^β (β>4/3), allowing vacuum and large initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior results on compressible nematic liquid crystal flows often require small initial data or geometric angle conditions on the initial orientation field. This work aims to remove such restrictions and handle vacuum.

Method: Analysis of strong solutions in periodic or bounded simply connected 2D domains, with constant shear viscosity and bulk viscosity λ(ρ)=ρ^β, β>4/3.

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions for large data with vacuum; no geometric angle condition; time-uniform density upper bound; exponential decay toward equilibrium.

Conclusion: The results establish robust global well-posedness and large-time behavior for these flows without size or geometric restrictions.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the global existence and large-time behavior of strong solutions to two-dimensional compressible nematic liquid crystal flows in the periodic domain or in a bounded simply connected domain. The shear viscosity is assumed to be a positive constant, while the bulk viscosity is given by $λ(ρ)=ρ^β$ with $β>4/3$. For initial data allowing vacuum, we establish the global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions without any restrictions on the size of the initial data. In particular, no geometric angle condition is imposed on the initial orientation field. Moreover, we derive a time-uniform upper bound for the density and establish the exponential decay of the strong solutions toward equilibrium.

</details>
