### [89] [Global Well-Posedness of Strong Solutions to the Two-Dimensional Compressible Nematic Liquid Crystal Flows with Large Initial Data and Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23770)
*Qinghao Lei,Lu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global well-posedness of 2D compressible nematic liquid crystal flows in whole/half space with bulk viscosity λ(ρ)=ρ^β (β>4/3), strong solutions without smallness restrictions and allowing vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish global existence and uniqueness for 2D compressible nematic liquid crystal flows under weak assumptions on bulk viscosity, removing prior constraints such as small initial data, absence of vacuum, or geometric angle conditions on the orientation field.

Method: Analysis of the compressible nematic liquid crystal system with constant shear viscosity and density-dependent bulk viscosity λ(ρ)=ρ^β (β>4/3), in whole space or half-space, proving global-in-time strong solutions.

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions established for β>4/3, without any restriction on initial data size, allowing vacuum, and without requiring any geometric angle condition on the initial orientation field.

Conclusion: The paper provides a general global well-posedness theory for 2D compressible nematic liquid crystal flows, significantly relaxing prior conditions on initial data, vacuum, and orientation field geometry.

Abstract: This paper concerns the global well-posedness of two-dimensional compressible nematic liquid crystal flows in the whole space or in the half-space. Under the assumptions that the shear viscosity is a positive constant and that the bulk viscosity is given by $λ(ρ)=ρ^β$ with $β>4/3$, we establish the global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions. It should be mentioned that our results are obtained without any restrictions on the size of the initial data and allow for the presence of vacuum. In particular, the initial orientation field is not required to satisfy any geometric angle condition.

</details>


### [90] [Uniqueness and Nondegeneracy of Ground State for the $L^2$-Critical Boson Star Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23771)
*Pan Chen,Qi Guo,Juncheng Wei,Yuanyang Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves uniqueness (up to phase rotations and translations) of the ground state for the L^2-critical boson star equation in R^3, determines the kernels of the real and imaginary linearized operators, and thereby confirms the Lieb-Yau uniqueness conjecture for masses near the Chandrasekhar mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Lieb-Yau uniqueness conjecture concerns the ground state of the boson star equation, but a full proof has remained open. This work aims to resolve it in the regime where the mass is sufficiently close to the Chandrasekhar mass.

Method: The authors study the L^2-critical boson star equation (sqrt(-Delta)+1)Q = (|x|^{-1}*|Q|^2)Q on R^3. They prove uniqueness of the ground state up to phase rotations and translations, and analyze the real and imaginary linearized operators around it to determine their kernels.

Result: Uniqueness of the ground state is established up to phase rotations and translations. The kernels of the real and imaginary linearized operators are explicitly determined. Consequently, the Lieb-Yau uniqueness conjecture is shown to hold when the mass is sufficiently close to the Chandrasekhar mass.

Conclusion: The Lieb-Yau uniqueness conjecture holds in the near-Chandrasekhar-mass regime, and the linearized operator analysis provides the key structural information underpinning this result.

Abstract: We prove uniqueness, up to phase rotations and translations, of the ground state for the $L^2$-critical boson star equation \[
  (\sqrt{-Δ}+1)Q
  =\bigl(|x|^{-1}* |Q|^2\bigr)Q,
  \qquad x\in\mathbb R^3. \] We also determine the kernels of the real and imaginary linearized operators. As a consequence, we show the Lieb-Yau uniqueness conjecture holds when the mass is sufficiently close to the Chandrasekhar mass.

</details>


### [91] [On rigidity of stationary black holes under a nontrapping assumption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23872)
*Alexandru F. Radu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A smooth, regular, asymptotically flat stationary vacuum black hole with no trapped null geodesic orthogonal to the stationary Killing field must have a nonextremal Kerr exterior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify stationary vacuum black holes as Kerr under a physically motivated nontrapping condition, extending rigidity results without assuming axisymmetry a priori.

Method: Extends local rotational symmetry via successive domains; if the limiting boundary in the ergoregion is nonempty it is locally a timelike photon surface for the stationary quotient metric. Derives uniform estimates along complete rotational orbits from nontrapping, uses convexity along short null segments, and lifts null geodesics to spacetime geodesics orthogonal to the stationary field; circularity continues them through ergosurface contact.

Result: The trapped-orbit-free condition contradicts the existence of a nonempty limiting ergoregion boundary, yielding global axisymmetry and hence a nonextremal Kerr exterior.

Conclusion: Nontrapping of null geodesics orthogonal to the stationary Killing field forces global axisymmetry, establishing that such black holes are nonextremal Kerr.

Abstract: We prove that a smooth, regular, asymptotically flat stationary vacuum black hole has a nonextremal Kerr exterior if no null geodesic orthogonal to its stationary Killing field is trapped. We extend the local rotational symmetry by choosing successive domains whose limiting boundary in the ergoregion, if nonempty, is locally a timelike photon surface for the stationary quotient metric. To obtain this geometry without an initial regularity assumption on the boundary, we derive uniform estimates along complete rotational orbits from nontrapping and use convexity along short null segments to control the boundary. Its null geodesics lift to spacetime null geodesics orthogonal to the stationary field. Circularity allows us to continue them through ergosurface contact with stationary projection confined to a compact set, contradicting nontrapping and giving global axisymmetry.

</details>


### [92] [The uniform-in-time electrostatic limit of the linearised Vlasov-Maxwell system: the Poisson equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23908)
*Marnie Smith*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the linearised Vlasov-Maxwell system around the Poisson equilibrium in R^3 in the large speed-of-light regime, showing that the perturbation stays O(c^{-1})-close to the linearised Vlasov-Poisson dynamics uniformly in time, with sharp rate in c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the Newtonian (non-relativistic) limit of kinetic plasma dynamics is central, and one wants to know whether magnetic/transverse effects alter the well-known Landau damping picture of Vlasov-Poisson. A rigorous comparison between Vlasov-Maxwell and Vlasov-Poisson in the large-c regime is needed.

Method: Linearise the Vlasov-Maxwell system with Newtonian particle transport about the Poisson equilibrium on R^3, treating c as a large parameter and allowing order-one transverse fields. Analyse the longitudinal dynamics via the same Volterra equation as in linearised Vlasov-Poisson, and handle transverse fields through Klein-Gordon-type dispersion, controlling their accumulated effect along free transport to obtain a uniform-in-time comparison.

Result: For uniformly controlled initial data, the difference between the Vlasov-Maxwell perturbation and its linearised Vlasov-Poisson counterpart is bounded uniformly in time by their initial discrepancy plus O(c^{-1}). The same estimate holds for scattering states, and the power of c^{-1} is sharp.

Conclusion: The large-c limit of linearised Vlasov-Maxwell is well approximated by linearised Vlasov-Poisson: longitudinal modes are governed by the same Volterra equation and undergo Landau damping, while transverse (Klein-Gordon-type) fields contribute only a sharp O(c^{-1}) correction to the uniform-in-time and scattering comparison.

Abstract: The Vlasov-Maxwell system with Newtonian particle transport is linearised about the Poisson equilibrium on $\mathbb{R}^3$, with the speed of light $c$ treated as a large parameter. For uniformly controlled initial data, with transverse fields that may remain of order one, the difference between the Vlasov-Maxwell perturbation and its linearised Vlasov-Poisson counterpart is bounded uniformly in time by their initial discrepancy plus a term of order $c^{-1}$. The same estimate holds for their scattering states, and the rate in $c$ is sharp. The longitudinal dynamics are governed by the same Volterra equation as in linearised Vlasov-Poisson and undergo Landau damping. The transverse fields exhibit Klein-Gordon-type dispersion, and control of their accumulated effect along free transport yields the uniform comparison.

</details>


### [93] [Improved leading coefficient in the individual Berezin-Li-Yau bound via energy orthogonality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23923)
*Yifan Wang,Hehu Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper strictly improves the leading coefficient in the individual Dirichlet eigenvalue bound beyond the Berezin–Li–Yau value n/(n+2), achieving an explicit constant c_n with n/(n+2) < c_n < 1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The direct individual consequence of the Berezin–Li–Yau sum inequality has leading coefficient n/(n+2) relative to the Weyl term. The authors aim to strictly improve this coefficient for individual eigenvalues without boundary regularity assumptions.

Method: Energy orthogonality provides a frequency-dependent cap on the Fourier density of the first k eigenfunctions. This cap is combined with the standard Bessel bound and a bathtub principle for the radial capacity. The resulting constants are characterized by explicit scalar equations.

Result: For every k≥1 and n≥2, λ_k ≥ c_n (2π)^2 ω_n^{-2/n} |Ω|^{-2/n} k^{2/n} with n/(n+2) < c_n < 1. In dimension two, c_2 = 0.5383068077…, a 7.66% improvement over the individual Li–Yau coefficient.

Conclusion: The leading coefficient in the individual eigenvalue bound is strictly improved, and the new constant c_n is independent of geometry and index k.

Abstract: Let $λ_k$ be the $k$th eigenvalue of the Dirichlet Laplacian on an open set $Ω\subset\mathbb R^n$ of finite positive measure. The direct individual consequence of the Berezin--Li--Yau sum inequality has leading coefficient $n/(n+2)$ relative to the Weyl term. The main contribution of this paper is a strict improvement of this coefficient. Energy orthogonality gives a frequency-dependent cap on the Fourier density of the first $k$ eigenfunctions. Combining this cap with the standard Bessel bound and a bathtub principle for the radial capacity yields \[
  λ_k\geq c_n(2π)^2ω_n^{-2/n}
  |Ω|^{-2/n}k^{2/n},
  \qquad \frac{n}{n+2}<c_n<1, \] for every $k\geq1$ and every $n\geq2$, without boundary regularity. The constants are characterized by explicit scalar equations; in dimension two, $c_2=0.5383068077\ldots$, giving a $7.66\%$ improvement over the individual Li--Yau coefficient. We emphasize that this improves the leading coefficient in the individual eigenvalue bound and the new constant $c_n$ is independent of the geometry and index $k$.

</details>


### [94] [Vanishing discount and viscosity selection problems for mechanical Hamilton--Jacobi equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24009)
*Hiroyoshi Mitake,Panrui Ni,Hung V. Tran*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies simultaneous vanishing discount and viscosity limits for mechanical Hamilton–Jacobi equations on the torus, identifying the selected limit across critical, subcritical, and supercritical regimes and proving a concentration phenomenon for associated Gibbs measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the combined vanishing discount and viscosity limits for mechanical Hamilton–Jacobi equations is a nontrivial selection problem, especially the critical regime where the limiting behavior depends on delicate features of the potential.

Method: The authors analyze the coupled limits on the n-dimensional torus, using the critical Mañé potential and the quadratic behavior of the potential near its wells to explicitly characterize the selected limit and derive convergence estimates.

Result: In the critical regime, the selected limit is explicitly identified; sharp and quantitative convergence estimates are obtained in the subcritical regime; convergence is proven in the supercritical regime when the local harmonic ground-state energy has a unique minimizer among the wells; and a concentration phenomenon of the Gibbs measures is established.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete characterization of simultaneous vanishing discount and viscosity limits across all regimes, along with sharp estimates and a Gibbs measure concentration result.

Abstract: We study simultaneous vanishing discount and viscosity limits for mechanical Hamilton--Jacobi equations on the $n$-dimensional torus. In the critical regime, we explicitly identify the selected limit in terms of the critical Mañé potential and the quadratic behavior of the potential near its wells. We also obtain quantitative and sharp convergence estimates in the subcritical regime. In the supercritical regime, we prove convergence when the local harmonic ground-state energy of the potential has a unique minimizer among the wells. Finally, we establish a concentration phenomenon of the associated Gibbs measures.

</details>


### [95] [Monopolist nonlinear pricing in a seller's market](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24011)
*Lucas D. O'Brien*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The Rochet-Choné monopolist pricing model is analyzed in the seller's market limit; a monotonicity formula gives an asymptotic description of solutions, and the share of excluded consumers vanishes as demand grows, contradicting Armstrong's "desirability of exclusion."


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand monopolist screening/price discrimination in the limit of large consumer demand, and to test whether exclusion of consumers remains optimal (Armstrong's desirability of exclusion).

Method: Study the Rochet-Choné model in the seller's market limit; introduce a monotonicity formula to derive an asymptotic description of the optimal pricing/solution structure.

Result: The monotonicity formula yields an asymptotic description of solutions, and the proportion of consumers priced out of the market tends to zero as overall consumer demand increases.

Conclusion: In the seller's market limit, exclusion becomes negligible, providing a counterpoint to Armstrong's desirability of exclusion.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the Rochet-Choné model of monopolist pricing in the seller's market limit. We introduce a monotonicity formula which yields an asymptotic description of solutions, and prove that the proportion of consumers priced out of the market vanishes as overall consumer demand increases, providing a counterpoint to Armstrong's desirability of exclusion.

</details>


### [96] [Selection of critical solutions in non-monotone vanishing discount problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24032)
*Panrui Ni,Jun Yan,Maxime Zavidovique*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves uniform convergence of the maximal viscosity solution of a generalized vanishing discount Hamilton--Jacobi problem without monotonicity assumptions, characterizing the limit via the Peierls barrier and Mather measures, and extending to a variant where the strict subsolution hypothesis holds automatically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical vanishing discount problems for Hamilton--Jacobi equations rely on monotonicity (pointwise or in the averaged sense over all Mather measures), which restricts the Hamiltonian forms and the possible limiting critical solutions that can be selected. The authors aim to remove this monotonicity requirement and thereby enable selection of multiple critical solutions.

Method: Consider the discounted equation λa(x)u + H(x,Du) = c_0, assuming for each small λ>0 the existence of a strict subsolution bounded uniformly from below. Impose a smallness assumption on the Mather quotient and a sign condition on the discount coefficient a(x) determined by a prescribed part of the Aubry set. Use viscosity solution theory, the Peierls barrier, and selected Mather measures to analyze and characterize the limit.

Result: Under these assumptions, the maximal viscosity solution converges uniformly as λ→0+. The limit is characterized explicitly through the Peierls barrier and the selected Mather measures. The elementary solution of the critical equation H(x,Du)=c_0 for any isolated static class is realized as such a vanishing discount limit.

Conclusion: This provides the first mechanism for selecting multiple critical solutions in vanishing discount problems beyond the classical monotonicity framework, showing different families of Mather measures yield different limiting critical solutions. A variant of the discounted problem is proposed in which the strict subsolution hypothesis holds automatically, applying to more general Hamiltonians H and functions a.

Abstract: We study a generalized vanishing discount problem for Hamilton--Jacobi equations without assuming monotonicity either pointwise or in the averaged sense with respect to all Mather measures. Specifically, we consider \[ λa(x)u(x)+H\big(x,Du(x)\big)=c_0, \] and assume that, for every sufficiently small $λ>0$, this equation admits a strict subsolution bounded from below uniformly with respect to $λ$. We prove that, under a smallness assumption on the Mather quotient, the maximal viscosity solution converges uniformly as $λ\to 0^+$ under a sign condition on the discount coefficient $a(x)$ determined by a prescribed part of the Aubry set. The limit is characterized explicitly in terms of the Peierls barrier and the selected Mather measures. As an application, the elementary solution of the critical Hamilton--Jacobi equation \[ H\big(x,Du(x)\big)=c_0 \] associated with any isolated static class can be realized as the vanishing discount limit. This provides the first mechanism for selecting multiple critical solutions in vanishing discount problems beyond the classical monotonicity framework, showing that different families of Mather measures yield different limiting critical solutions.
  We finally propose a variant of the discounted problems for which the strict subsolution hypothesis is automatically verified and hence applies to more general Hamiltonians $H$ and functions $a$.

</details>


### [97] [Multiple solutions for planar Schrodinger-Poisson system with logarithmic nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24043)
*Wei Long,Changchang Yan,Jianghua Ye*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of a nontrivial solution, a ground state solution, and infinitely many non-radial sign-changing solutions for a planar Schrödinger-Poisson system with logarithmic nonlinearity and coercive potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The logarithmic nonlinearity makes the manifold method of Ruiz inapplicable, so a new approach is needed to establish solution existence for this planar Schrödinger-Poisson system.

Method: A general minimax principle is applied to an auxiliary functional to prove existence of a nontrivial solution. The energy functional is then minimized over the set of all nontrivial solutions to obtain a ground state solution. Under radial symmetry of V, infinitely many non-radial sign-changing solutions are derived.

Result: The system admits a nontrivial solution and a ground state solution. When V is radially symmetric, infinitely many non-radial sign-changing solutions exist.

Conclusion: The system admits a ground state solution and, under radial symmetry of the potential, infinitely many non-radial sign-changing solutions, overcoming the limitation of Ruiz's manifold method.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the following planar Schrodinger-Poisson system: $\begin{cases} -Δu +V(x)u+λφu=u\ln u^2, & \text{in }{R}^2,\\ Δφ=u^2, & \text{in }{R}^2. \end{cases}$ where $λ\in{R}$ is a parameter and $V\in C^1({R}^2,R}^{+})$ is a coercive potential. Due to the presence of the logarithmic nonlinearity, the manifold method developed by Ruiz [R] is not applicable here. We apply a general minimax principle to an auxiliary functional and prove the existence of a nontrivial solution. Then, by minimizing the energy functional over the set of all nontrivial solutions, we succeed in showing that the system admits a ground state solution. If, in addition, $V$ is radially symmetric, we obtain infinitely many non radial sign-changing solutions.

</details>


### [98] [Determinantal capacity and $L^\infty$-Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24078)
*Hao Fang,Biao Ma,Jinyang Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces "determinantal ellipticity" (det-ellipticity), a structural condition linking L∞-estimates for fully nonlinear elliptic operators on compact Kähler manifolds to algebraic/combinatorial properties of Hessian elliptic operators, via new D-subsolutions and Gårding polynomial characterizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fully nonlinear elliptic operators on compact Kähler manifolds lack a unified link between analytic L∞-estimates and algebraic/combinatorial structure of Hessian elliptic operators. The paper aims to provide such a bridge through a quantitative structural condition.

Method: Analytically, introduces D-subsolutions extending Sui–Sun's determinant sublevel condition, using the Guo–Phong–Tong auxiliary comparison method and pluripotential theory for complex Monge–Ampère equations to derive relative L∞-estimates in big cohomology classes under a determinant-entropy bound. Algebraically, studies Gårding elliptic polynomial operators of degree d, characterizing the determinant increment bound with exponent d/n via positivity of the determinantal capacity of the top homogeneous part.

Result: Obtains relative L∞-estimates in big cohomology classes for viscosity supersolutions and singular reference potentials under a determinant-entropy bound. Characterizes the determinant increment bound by determinantal capacity positivity, equivalent to balanced-point conditions for Newton polytopes of rank-one scalarizations and slope semistability of an associated subspace polymatroid.

Conclusion: Det-ellipticity systematically constructs the required subsolutions, and the algebraic criteria combined with analytic hypotheses yield relative L∞-estimates for the corresponding fully nonlinear equations, unifying analytic and algebraic perspectives.

Abstract: We introduce determinantal ellipticity, or det-ellipticity, a quantitative structural condition for fully nonlinear elliptic operators on compact Kähler manifolds that provides the link between the $L^\infty$-estimates and algebraic/combinatorial properties of a large class of Hessian elliptic operators.
  On the analytic side, we introduce $\mathcal D$-subsolutions extending the determinant sublevel condition of Sui--Sun. Using the auxiliary comparison method of Guo--Phong--Tong and pluripotential theory for complex Monge--Ampère equations, we obtain relative $L^\infty$-estimates in big cohomology classes under a determinant-entropy bound for viscosity supersolutions and singular reference potentials. Det-ellipticity provides a systematic construction of such subsolutions.
  On the algebraic side, we study Gårding elliptic polynomial operators of degree $d$. We characterize the determinant increment bound with exponent $d/n$ by positivity of the determinantal capacity of the top homogeneous part. This is equivalent to balanced-point conditions for the Newton polytopes of rank-one scalarizations and to slope semistability of an associated subspace polymatroid. Together with the analytic hypotheses, these criteria yield relative $L^\infty$ estimates for the corresponding fully nonlinear equations.

</details>


### [99] [Structure of radial solutions to a Hénon type equation with exponential nonlinearity on the hyperbolic space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24179)
*Yahui Jiang,Xianmei Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper classifies radial solution structures and establishes sharp separation/stability alternatives for a Hénon-type equation with exponential nonlinearity on hyperbolic space, for all n≥2 and α>-2, answering an open question by Huang and Zhao.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The properties of radial solutions to a Hénon-type equation with exponential nonlinearity on hyperbolic space were not fully understood, particularly their separation and stability across the full parameter range, and an open question recently posed by Huang and Zhao remained unresolved.

Method: Transform the radial hyperbolic equation into a weighted Euclidean equation, then apply well-established Euclidean separation results to analyze the solution structures.

Result: Sharp alternatives and a classification of radial solution structures are obtained across the full range n≥2 and α>-2, and the open question of Huang and Zhao is answered affirmatively.

Conclusion: The reduction to a weighted Euclidean problem is effective for studying radial hyperbolic Hénon-type equations, yielding complete separation and stability results and resolving the posed open problem.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the separation and stability properties of radial solutions to a Hénon-type equation with exponential nonlinearity on the hyperbolic space. By transforming the radial hyperbolic equation into a weighted Euclidean equation and applying well-established separation results in Euclidean space, we classify the solution structures or establish sharp alternatives for radial solutions throughout the full range $n\ge2$ and $α>-2$. We also give an affirmative answer to the open question recently posed by Huang and Zhao.

</details>


### [100] [Regular Solutions of the Stationary Navier-Stokes Equations in Arbitrarily High Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24206)
*Wendong Wang,Guoxu Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper removes the upper dimension restriction (n≤15) on the existence of regular solutions to the stationary incompressible Navier–Stokes equations with bounded compactly supported forces, establishing existence in arbitrarily high dimensions n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Li–Yang (2022) proved existence of regular solutions for 5≤n≤15, but the case n>15 remained open. This paper aims to answer that question and extend existence to all high dimensions.

Method: Combines an averaged signed pressure-potential identity with L²-BMO estimates for the Bernoulli source flux, giving a Bernoulli supremum bound independent of the skew coefficient (drift) size. Together with the energy estimate for the comparison solution, this yields a sublinear bound for the Newtonian potential of the positive Bernoulli function.

Result: Establishes existence of regular solutions in arbitrarily high dimensions. Also constructs periodic and whole-space solutions for forces in L∞∩L^{2n/(n+2)}, and for compactly supported forces records two far-field profiles with velocity, gradient, and pressure remainders plus cancellation criteria.

Conclusion: The sublinear bound removes the dimension restriction, proving regular solutions exist for stationary Navier–Stokes in any dimension. Additional results cover periodic/whole-space solutions and far-field asymptotics.

Abstract: Recently, the existence of regular solutions $(u,p)$ to the stationary incompressible Navier--Stokes equations on $\mathbb R^n$ with bounded compactly supported forces $f$ for $5\le n\le15$ was established by Li--Yang (Comm. Math. Phys., 2022). However, it is still unknown whether there exists a regular solution for dimensions $n>15$. Here we answer this question and obtain the existence of regular solutions to the stationary Navier--Stokes equations in arbitrarily high dimensions. The key innovation is to combine an averaged signed pressure-potential identity with $L^2$-BMO estimates for the Bernoulli source flux, yielding a Bernoulli supremum bound independent of the size of the skew coefficient representing the drift. Together with the energy estimate for the comparison solution, this yields a sublinear bound for the Newtonian potential of the positive Bernoulli function, removing the upper dimension restriction in the earlier existence argument. We also construct periodic solutions and whole-space solutions for forces in $L^\infty\cap L^{2n/(n+2)}$. For compactly supported forces, we record two far-field profiles, their velocity, gradient and pressure remainders, and the corresponding cancellation criteria.

</details>


### [101] [Rigidity of Landau solution in the rotated self-similar class](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24261)
*Yuxuan Shi,Liqun Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves rigidity of the Landau solution of the stationary Navier-Stokes equations within the rotated self-similar class when the rotation parameter α is sufficiently small or large.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Landau solution is central to stationary Navier-Stokes problems such as asymptotic behavior and regularity, motivating a study of its rigidity under self-similar symmetries.

Method: Two approaches: for small rotation, a compactness argument with a classification lemma for the self-similar kernel of the linearization around any Landau solution (extendable to DSS/RDSS); for large rotation, strong angular dissipation yields extra coercivity on the non-axisymmetric part.

Result: Rigidity of the Landau solution in the rotated self-similar class for α sufficiently small or large is established.

Conclusion: The Landau solution is rigid in the rotated self-similar class for small or large rotation parameters, with distinct mechanisms governing each regime.

Abstract: Landau solution is a special family of solutions to the stationary Navier Stokes equations, which is important for the study of stationary problems such as asymptotic behavior and regularity. In this paper we prove that the Landau solution is rigid in rotated self-similar class when rotation parameter $α$ sufficiently small or large. The arguments in the two parts rely on different approaches. For small rotation, we use the compactness argument and establish a classification lemma of the self-similar kernel of the linearization around any Landau solution, this method can also be extended to DSS and RDSS cases. For large rotation, the strong angular dissipation produces additional coercivity on the non-axisymmetric component, which forces the solution to reduce into Landau solution.

</details>


### [102] [The Outer Pressure Problem for the Compressible Navier--Stokes System with Temperature-Dependent Transport Coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24416)
*Hongyu Wang,Rong Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies global strong solutions and large-time behavior for the 1D outer pressure problem of a viscous, heat-conducting ideal polytropic gas with temperature-dependent viscosity μ=μ̃θ^α and heat conductivity κ=κ̃θ^β, allowing large H^1 data when α≥0 is small.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding global well-posedness and long-time asymptotics of compressible, heat-conducting gas flow with temperature-dependent transport coefficients under outer pressure boundary conditions.

Method: Analysis of the 1D outer pressure problem with constitutive laws μ=μ̃θ^α, κ=κ̃θ^β; imposing prescribed nonnegative pressure and weighted pressure conditions; handling large H^1 initial data with positive specific volume and temperature.

Result: For each nonnegative pressure and fixed β≥0, global strong solutions exist for large H^1 data provided α≥0 is sufficiently small. With positive limiting outer pressure, solutions have uniform bounds and converge to a stationary state. With zero limiting pressure under weighted pressure conditions, the normalized solution converges to an expanding state at algebraic rate in physical time.

Conclusion: Global strong solutions exist and exhibit distinct large-time behavior depending on the limiting outer pressure, establishing stability to stationary or expanding states.

Abstract: We study global strong solutions and their large-time behavior for the one-dimensional outer pressure problem of a viscous, heat-conducting ideal polytropic gas. The viscosity and heat conductivity satisfy $μ=\tildeμθ^α$ and $κ=\tildeκθ^β$. For each prescribed nonnegative pressure and each fixed $β\geq0$, we allow large $H^1$ initial data with positive specific volume and temperature, provided that $α\geq0$ is sufficiently small. When the limiting outer pressure is positive, the solution has uniform bounds and converges to a stationary state. When the limiting outer pressure is zero and the stated weighted pressure conditions hold, the normalized solution converges to an expanding state at an algebraic rate in physical time.

</details>


### [103] [Initial and initial boundary value problems for the Navier-Stokes equations with temperature-dependent transport coefficients and large data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24435)
*Hongyu Wang,Rong Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence and asymptotic stability of strong solutions to 1D compressible Navier-Stokes equations with temperature-dependent viscosity and heat conductivity, for large H1 initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish well-posedness and stability for compressible Navier-Stokes flows where transport coefficients depend on temperature in unbounded domains, removing restrictive initial regularity assumptions.

Method: Considers viscosity and heat conductivity proportional to θ^α and θ^β. For β≥0 and sufficiently small α≥0, proves global existence of strong solutions for arbitrarily large H1 data by dividing the momentum equation by the viscosity, enabling estimation of the specific-volume derivative without an initial H2 assumption.

Result: Global existence and asymptotic stability of a strong solution for arbitrarily large H1 initial data, in both initial and initial-boundary value problems.

Conclusion: Temperature-dependent viscosity/conductivity still permits global strong solutions under mild parameter restrictions, and the technical trick avoids needing H2 initial data.

Abstract: We consider initial and initial boundary value problems for the one-dimensional compressible Navier--Stokes equations of an ideal polytropic gas in unbounded domains. The viscosity and heat conductivity are proportional to $θ^α$ and $θ^β$, respectively. For every fixed $β\geq0$ and sufficiently small $α\geq0$, depending on the initial data, we prove the global existence and asymptotic stability of a strong solution for arbitrarily large $H^1$ initial data. Dividing the momentum equation by the viscosity allows us to estimate the specific-volume derivative without an initial $H^2$ assumption.

</details>


### [104] [Higher-order estimates of highest waves of the Whitham equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24447)
*Robin Østern Lien*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper confirms a conjecture on higher-order asymptotic expansions at the crest for Whitham-type nonlocal nonlinear equations with highest waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ehrnström, Maehlen and Varholm established leading-order asymptotics at the crest of solutions to Whitham-type equations and conjectured similar expansions for all derivatives. This work aims to prove that conjecture.

Method: The authors extend Ehrnström et al.'s techniques via strong induction, redistributing difference operators to handle singularities in higher-order derivatives caused by integral convolution kernels. A uniform sign estimate is verified using rigorous interval arithmetic for the zeroth-order asymptotics.

Result: The conjecture is confirmed for a range of equations with highest waves of Hölder regularity C^s for s in [0.35, 1). The restriction s >= 0.35 stems only from the zeroth-order argument, while the higher-order induction holds for all s in (0, 1).

Conclusion: Any extension of the zeroth-order result would immediately yield the corresponding higher-order expansions, since the induction itself applies for all s in (0,1).

Abstract: The Whitham equation has given its name to a wider family of nonlocal, nonlinear equations with very weak dispersion, which all feature highest waves. Recent work by Ehrnström, Maehlen and Varholm establishes leading-order asymptotics at the crest of solutions to such Whitham-type equations, and conjectures similar expansions for all derivatives of the solution. By extending their techniques we confirm the conjecture for a range of equations with highest waves of Hölder regularity $C^{s}$ for $s\in[0.35,1)$. We do this by strong induction, redistributing difference operators to deal with singularities in higher-order derivatives arising from integral convolution kernels. The restriction $s\geq0.35$ arises solely from the separate argument establishing the zeroth-order asymptotics, which requires a uniform sign estimate that we verify using rigorous interval arithmetic. The higher-order induction itself applies for every $s\in(0,1)$, so any extension of the zeroth-order result immediately yields the corresponding higher-order expansions.

</details>


### [105] [Geometric flows of branched transportation networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24451)
*Julius Lohmann,Yoshihiro Tonegawa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a geometric flow for branched transportation networks by adapting Brakke's variational approximation scheme, proving existence of a flat-norm Hölder continuous limit with decreasing transport cost, and analyzing a related 1-varifold flow with rectifiability and a Brakke-type motion law.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Branched transport is a nonconvex, nonsmooth variational problem producing hierarchically ramified networks with regularity resembling singular surfaces in Brakke flow. The authors seek to bridge branched transport and mean curvature flow via a geometric evolution of transportation networks.

Method: They modify Brakke's variational approximation scheme to construct a geometric flow of transportation networks represented as normal real 1-rectifiable currents, and separately analyze a related flow approximated by 1-varifolds whose weight measures model the branched transport cost.

Result: They prove existence of a limit that is Hölder continuous with respect to the flat norm and whose branched transport cost decreases along the evolution. For the 1-varifold flow, they establish 1-rectifiability and a motion law analogous to Brakke's inequality.

Conclusion: The work connects branched transport with curvature-driven flows, yielding a well-posed geometric evolution for ramified networks with monotone decreasing cost and a varifold formulation satisfying a Brakke-type inequality.

Abstract: The branched transport problem is a nonconvex and nonsmooth variational optimization problem on normal $1$-currents in $\mathbb{R}^n$ with prescribed boundary. The optimality is with respect to some non-decreasing, lower semicontinuous, and subadditive function $τ:\mathbb{R}_+\to\mathbb{R}_+$ with $τ(0)=0$ describing the cost $τ(m)$ to move an amount of mass $m$ per unit distance. The subadditivity leads to complicated, hierarchically ramified patterns in the support of (suboptimal) solutions. These network-like sets appear to have regularity properties similar to those of the singular surfaces arising in the so-called Brakke flow, a weak generalization of the mean curvature flow. We construct a geometric flow of transportation networks, which correspond to normal real $1$-rectifiable currents, by modifying Brakke's variational approximation scheme. We prove the existence of a limit that is Hölder continuous with respect to the flat norm and whose branched transport cost decreases along the geometric evolution. We further analyze a closely related geometric flow approximated by $1$-varifolds, whose weight measures model the branched transport cost, and establish its $1$-rectifiability together with a motion law analogous to Brakke's inequality.

</details>


### [106] [Charge-free well-posedness and charge-driven norm inflation for the Euler-Maxwell system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24481)
*Haroune Houamed*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the 2D incompressible Euler-Maxwell system under normal geometry, distinguishing the projected (charge-free) formulation from the unprojected one. The projected system admits local well-posedness for H^1 velocities with bounded vorticity, while the unprojected system exhibits vorticity norm inflation from the longitudinal electric mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the Gauss constraint (charge-free projection) affects regularity of the 2D incompressible Euler-Maxwell system under normal geometry, and to contrast projected vs unprojected dynamics.

Method: Uses an endpoint time-like trace estimate for half-waves controlling the magnetic gradient in L_t^2 along fluid trajectories, closing the Yudovich vorticity estimate without an Eulerian Lipschitz bound on the electromagnetic field. For the unprojected system, constructs smooth compactly supported radial electromagnetic data.

Result: Projected system: local well-posedness for velocities in H^1 with bounded vorticity and electromagnetic data in H^{3/2}, under a strict sub-luminal condition. Unprojected system: smooth radial data converging to zero in H^{3/2} with zero initial velocity yield unique global smooth solutions with vorticity L^infty norm inflation.

Conclusion: At the same electromagnetic Sobolev regularity, the Gauss constraint separates endpoint regularity propagation from charge-driven vorticity norm inflation.

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional incompressible Euler-Maxwell system under the normal geometry in which the velocity and electric field are planar and the magnetic field is normal to the plane. We identify a structural distinction between the projected, charge-free formulation and the unprojected system.
  For the projected system, we establish a local well-posedness theory for initial velocities in $H^1(\mathbb R^2)$ with bounded initial vorticity and electromagnetic data in $H^{\frac 32}(\mathbb R^2)$, under a strict sub-luminal condition on the velocity. The main ingredient is an endpoint time-like trace estimate for half-waves, which controls the magnetic gradient in $L_t^2$ along every fluid trajectory. This closes the Yudovich vorticity estimate without requiring an Eulerian Lipschitz bound on the electromagnetic field.
  For the unprojected system, the longitudinal electric mode produces an additional vorticity source term containing the charge density $\operatorname{div }E$. In this case, we construct smooth, compactly supported radial electromagnetic data converging to zero in $H^{\frac32}(\mathbb R^2)$, with zero initial velocity, whose unique global smooth solution exhibits a vorticity norm inflation in $L^\infty_x$. Thus, at the same electromagnetic Sobolev regularity, the Gauss constraint separates endpoint regularity propagation from charge-driven vorticity norm inflation.

</details>


### [107] [Density Collapse and Gradient Catastrophe in a One-Dimensional Euler--Poisson--Cattaneo System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24546)
*Qingsong Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper examines finite-time singularity formation in a 1D Euler–Poisson system with Cattaneo heat conduction and quadratic heat-flux pressure/energy corrections, using Lagrangian coordinates with κ=κ₀v, and identifies two distinct blow-up mechanisms: density collapse and small-amplitude gradient catastrophe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to understand how heat conduction (via Cattaneo's law) and quadratic heat-flux corrections affect finite-time breakdown in an Euler–Poisson system, distinguishing different singularity mechanisms.

Method: Analysis in Lagrangian coordinates with structural choice κ=κ₀v; construction of an explicit affine solution; reformulation near equilibrium using F=φ_x/v as a strictly hyperbolic balance law with constraint F_x=1−v; computation of characteristic speeds and genuine-nonlinearity coefficient; a constraint-compatible John–Hörmander–Bärlin bootstrap plus Riccati comparison for compactly supported neutral data.

Result: Two breakdown mechanisms: (1) an explicit affine solution with v→0 in finite time causing Eulerian density ρ=1/v divergence while other variables stay finite; (2) for a'(1)<0, a narrow compressive wave produces only an O(ε²η) zero-speed Poisson mode, and the bootstrap/Riccati argument yields finite-time gradient catastrophe with the solution staying near equilibrium and F_x bounded. Genuine nonlinearity fails for all propagating families only under two simultaneous explicit algebraic degeneracy conditions.

Conclusion: Within the same Euler–Poisson–Cattaneo model, density collapse and small-amplitude gradient blow-up constitute two distinct finite-time breakdown mechanisms.

Abstract: We study finite-time singularity formation for a one-dimensional Euler--Poisson system in Lagrangian coordinates with Cattaneo heat conduction and quadratic heat-flux corrections in the pressure and internal energy. Under the structural choice $κ=κ_0v$, two different breakdown mechanisms are obtained. First, an explicit affine solution reaches $v=0$ in finite time, so the Eulerian density $ρ=1/v$ diverges while the remaining state variables stay finite at each fixed spatial point. Second, for $a'(1)<0$, the Poisson field is localized near equilibrium by $F=φ_x/v$, producing a strictly hyperbolic balance law subject to the propagated constraint $F_x=1-v$. We compute the characteristic speeds and an explicit genuine-nonlinearity coefficient and show that genuine nonlinearity fails for all propagating families only when two explicit algebraic degeneracy conditions hold simultaneously. For neutral compactly supported data satisfying the Poisson constraint exactly, a narrow compressive wave generates only an $O(\varepsilon^2η)$ zero-speed Poisson mode. A constraint-compatible John--Hörmander--Bärlin bootstrap and a Riccati comparison then yield finite-time gradient catastrophe while the solution remains uniformly close to equilibrium and $F_x$ stays bounded. The two results exhibit density collapse and small-amplitude gradient blow-up as distinct breakdown mechanisms within the same Euler--Poisson--Cattaneo model.

</details>


### [108] [Interior estimates for Hessian quotient equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24549)
*Guohuan Qiu,Jin Yan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes interior Hessian estimates for admissible solutions of constant right-hand side Hessian quotient equations when numerator and denominator degrees differ by one or two, and shows this range is optimal via counterexamples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hessian quotient equations are important in geometry and PDE theory, but interior Hessian estimates were not fully understood for general degree differences. This work addresses the gap by determining the exact degree range where such estimates hold.

Method: The proof combines a new nonlinear comparison function, a doubling maximum principle, and a Pogorelov estimate.

Result: Interior Hessian estimates are proven in arbitrary dimensions for the admissible solutions when the numerator and denominator differ in degree by one or two. Counterexamples demonstrate that this degree range is optimal.

Conclusion: The degree-difference condition of one or two is sharp for interior Hessian estimates of Hessian quotient equations with constant right-hand side.

Abstract: We prove interior Hessian estimates in arbitrary dimensions for admissible solutions of Hessian quotient equations with constant right-hand side, when the numerator and denominator differ in degree by one or two. Counterexamples show that this degree range is optimal. The proof combines a new nonlinear comparison function, a doubling maximum principle, and a Pogorelov estimate.

</details>


### [109] [The Boltzmann-BGK model with nontrivial collision frequency near vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24600)
*Sungsu Park,Seok-Bae Yun*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes global existence and uniqueness of mild solutions near vacuum for the 3D Boltzmann-BGK model with nontrivial collision frequency ν(f)=ρ^αT^β, without requiring uniform positive lower bounds on density or temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing well-posedness results for Boltzmann-BGK models with non-constant collision frequency were largely confined to near-equilibrium regimes and stationary problems. This work extends to near-vacuum solutions, avoiding assumptions of uniform positive lower bounds on macroscopic density or temperature.

Method: Two mechanisms: (1) a phase-space weight invariant along free-transport characteristics giving dispersive estimates for the collision frequency and gain term, with time-integrable decay controlling nonlinear growth; (2) a Lipschitz estimate for the relaxation operator νM in weighted L^1 space controlling mass and energy, where the collision frequency compensates for singular dependence on macroscopic fields.

Result: For nonnegative initial data with finite mass and energy, sufficiently small in suitable polynomial-weighted L^∞ norms, a unique global mild solution with uniform weighted bounds is obtained.

Conclusion: Global well-posedness near vacuum is established for the Boltzmann-BGK model with ν(f)=ρ^αT^β, α∈(1/3,1], β∈[0,1], 3α+2β≥2, without uniform positive lower bounds on density or temperature.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the Boltzmann-BGK model in the three-dimensional whole space with nontrivial collision frequency $ν(f)=ρ^αT^β$, where $α\in(1/3,1]$, $β\in[0,1]$, and $3α+2β\geq 2$. Existing well-posedness results for such models have largely been confined to near-equilibrium regimes and stationary problems. For nonnegative initial data with finite mass and energy that are sufficiently small in suitable polynomial-weighted $L^{\infty}$ norms, we establish global existence and uniqueness of mild solutions near vacuum. No uniform positive lower bound on the macroscopic density or temperature is imposed. The analysis relies on two complementary mechanisms. First, a phase-space weight invariant along free-transport characteristics yields dispersive estimates for the collision frequency and the gain term. The resulting time-integrable decay controls the nonlinear growth and closes the global weighted estimates. Second, we establish a Lipschitz estimate for the relaxation operator in a weighted $L^1$ space controlling mass and energy, with a Lipschitz constant depending only on weighted upper bounds for the distribution functions. The relaxation operator $ν\mathcal{M}$ has a more favorable structure for Lipschitz estimates than the local Maxwellian $\mathcal{M}$ alone: the collision frequency compensates for the singular dependence on the macroscopic fields, allowing us to establish Lipschitz continuity without uniform positive lower bounds for the density or temperature. Together, these estimates yield a unique global mild solution with uniform weighted bounds.

</details>


### [110] [Strict ground-level gaps on long-pendant generalized star graphs for nonlinear Dirac equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24618)
*Zhipeng Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that sufficiently long pendant paths in the essential reduction of a noncompact metric graph force a strict ground-level gap \(d_\lambda(\mathcal G)<d_\lambda^\infty\) for autonomous nonlinear Dirac equations, while identifying graph classes (pure transmission graphs) where no gap can exist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For nonlinear Dirac equations on noncompact metric graphs, the ground-level at infinity is governed by the full-line level \(d_\lambda^\infty\). Understanding when the graph ground level \(d_\lambda\) strictly drops below this level is central to detecting concentration and existence phenomena; the paper aims to characterize graph structures that guarantee such a gap.

Method: The proof combines a half-line restriction of a symmetric full-line ground state, uniform exponential decay estimates, resolvent localization for massive Dirac-Kirchhoff spectral projections, and a compactness argument for generalized Nehari fibres. Emphasis is placed on the essential reduction of the graph and the presence of a sufficiently long pendant path.

Result: Uniformly for \(\lambda\) in a compact subinterval \(I\Subset(-mc^2,mc^2)\), a sufficiently long pendant path in the essential reduction implies \(d_\lambda(\mathcal G)<d_\lambda^\infty\). Conversely, pure transmission graphs whose essential reduction is isometric to the full line satisfy \(d_\lambda(\mathcal G)=d_\lambda^\infty\), so no strict gap can occur.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a sharp structural criterion for strict ground-level gaps: pendant paths generate the gap, whereas pure transmission graphs with full-line essential reduction cannot. This delineates elementary graph classes where gap phenomena are absent.

Abstract: We study strict ground-level gaps for autonomous nonlinear Dirac equations on noncompact metric graphs. The relevant level at infinity is the autonomous full-line level \(d_λ^\infty=d_{λ,\mathbb R}\), since a concentrating sequence escaping along a half-line sees the real line after recentering far from all vertices. We prove that, uniformly for \(λ\) in a compact subinterval \(I\Subset(-mc^2,mc^2)\), the presence of a sufficiently long pendant path in the essential reduction of the graph implies \[ d_λ(\mathcal G)<d_λ^\infty. \] The proof uses a half-line restriction of a symmetric full-line ground state, uniform exponential decay, resolvent localization for massive Dirac-Kirchhoff spectral projections, and a compactness argument for generalized Nehari fibres. We also identify elementary graph classes for which no such gap can hold: pure transmission graphs whose essential reduction is isometric to the full line satisfy \(d_λ(\mathcal G)=d_λ^\infty\).

</details>


### [111] [Global stability of superposition of viscous contact wave and rarefaction waves for compressible Navier-Stokes system with temperature-dependent transport coefficients and large data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24700)
*Hongyu Wang,Rong Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global stability of the combination of a viscous contact wave with rarefaction waves for the 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes equations with temperature-dependent viscosity and heat conductivity, for small viscosity exponent α and small total wave strength, allowing large H^1 initial perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses the Cauchy problem for compressible Navier-Stokes with density- and temperature-dependent transport coefficients. Prior stability results for viscous contact waves combined with rarefaction waves often rely on constant coefficients or restrictive assumptions; here the viscosity μ(θ)=μ̃θ^α and heat conductivity κ(θ)=κ̃θ^β introduce additional nonlinearity that complicates the analysis.

Method: The authors consider the 1D full compressible Navier-Stokes system with μ(θ)=μ̃θ^α and κ(θ)=κ̃θ^β. For fixed β≥0, they use an energy/entropy method combined with wave decomposition (viscous contact wave plus rarefaction waves). Key steps include deriving time-uniform lower and upper bounds for specific volume and temperature, and controlling the logarithmic volume derivative and temperature gradient to close H^1 estimates without needing a second derivative of the initial specific volume.

Result: For each fixed β≥0, global stability is proved when the viscosity exponent α and total wave strength are sufficiently small. The initial perturbation may be large in H^1, provided specific volume and temperature have positive initial lower bounds. The solution remains uniformly bounded in H^1 relative to the wave and converges uniformly to the wave as time tends to infinity. Both specific volume and temperature admit time-uniform lower and upper bounds.

Conclusion: The results extend global stability theory for viscous contact plus rarefaction waves to the full compressible Navier-Stokes system with temperature-dependent viscosity and heat conductivity, under small α and small wave strength. Notably, H^1 closure is achieved without requiring a second derivative of the initial specific volume.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the one-dimensional full compressible Navier--Stokes equations with viscosity $μ(θ)=\tildeμθ^α$ and heat conductivity $κ(θ)=\tildeκθ^β$. For each fixed $β\ge0$, we prove the global stability of the combination of a viscous contact wave with rarefaction waves, provided that the viscosity exponent $α$ and the total wave strength are sufficiently small. The initial perturbation may be large in $H^1$, and the specific volume and temperature are assumed to have positive initial lower bounds. The solution remains uniformly bounded in $H^1$ relative to the wave, and converges uniformly to that wave as time tends to infinity. We show that both the specific volume and the temperature admit time-uniform lower and upper bound. Estimates for the logarithmic volume derivative and the temperature gradient then close the $H^1$ estimates without requiring a second derivative of the initial specific volume.

</details>


### [112] [Counterexamples to Grad's conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24739)
*Javier Gómez-Serrano,Lukas Liehr,Mitchell A. Taylor*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs smooth MHD equilibria on solid tori with exactly C_N symmetry (N large), nested toroidal pressure levels, and vanishing field only on a round magnetic axis, with a Lean 4 formalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grad conjectured that toroidal MHD equilibria should possess certain symmetries (plane-reflection, axial, or helical). This work aims to construct counterexamples to these conjectures, showing abundant equilibria with only discrete cyclic symmetry.

Method: Perturbative construction of smooth solutions to the magnetohydrostatic equations on embedded solid tori, yielding a one-parameter family of equilibria whose symmetry group is exactly the cyclic group C_N of rotations.

Result: For each sufficiently large N, smooth equilibria exist with nested toroidal pressure levels, magnetic field vanishing exactly on a round axis, and symmetry group exactly C_N (even admitting sign-reversing isometries). The equilibria form a locally nontrivial one-parameter family in the moduli space and lack all Grad-conjectured symmetries. A Lean 4 certification is provided.

Conclusion: The Grad symmetry conjectures are false in general; many toroidal MHD equilibria have only the minimal discrete rotational symmetry C_N, and these results are formally verified in Lean 4.

Abstract: For each sufficiently large $N$, we construct smooth solutions of the magnetohydrostatic equations on embedded solid tori whose regular pressure levels are nested tori, whose magnetic field vanishes exactly on a round magnetic axis, and whose group of Euclidean symmetries, even when isometries reversing the sign of the field are admitted, is exactly the cyclic group $C_N$ of rotations through multiples of $2π/N$. For each such $N$, these equilibria form a smooth one-parameter family which is locally nontrivial in the moduli space of embedded configurations and have none of the plane-reflection, axial, or helical symmetries conjectured by Grad. A Lean 4 certification of the above results is also provided.

</details>
